Champions League’s highest-scoring teenagers: Yamal, Mbappe, Bellingham…

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Neither Lionel Messi nor Cristiano Ronaldo feature among the highest-scoring teenagers in Champions League history.

That’s a surprising truth given their superhuman career achievements and the fact that so many football stars have used Europe’s premier club competition as a springboard for their blossoming career.

If you’re good enough, you’re old enough. Here are the male players to have scored the most goals as teenagers in the Champions League.

Now recognised more for his Barclaysman status, ‘the Yak’ actually has a unique place in Champions League history as one of the competition’s most prolific teenagers.

He found form in the Champions League upon signing for Maccabi Haifa, including a penalty against Manchester United and a hat-trick against Olympiakos. It was that form which prompted a move to England with Portsmouth.

Yakubu managed five goals in just five games before leaving his teenage years.

It feels like Bellingham has now been around scoring worldies and standing with his arms out beside him in celebration for years, when in actual fact he’s 21. Twenty-one.

He played 23 times as a teenager in the competition, scoring six goals from midfield with Borussia Dortmund after moving from Birmingham. He’s now in a tussle to win the Ballon d’Or with teammate Vinicius Junior. Time flies when you’re bloody good at football.

Real Madrid’s hometown hero, it somehow goes unappreciated as to exactly how good Raul was, for so long.

He scored six times in eight Champions League games as a teenager for Real Madrid, a swift start on his way to becoming the first player to score 50 goals in the competition and make over 100 appearances.

The legendary Frenchman burst onto the scene as a teenager at Lyon and that was represented in his start in the Champions League, bagging six goals from nine games before his teen years were over, including a goal on his competition debut.

It’s taken him a while to properly receive his flowers, but all these years later, Benzema is correctly remembered as one of the greatest modern forwards, playing facilitator to Cristiano Ronaldo and Gareth Bale at Real Madrid before becoming the main man and firing them to European glory himself in 2022.

Of the five Champions Leagues he won, his individual performances to clinch the final one make it the most memorable.

Seven goals from his 18 games as a teenager in the Champions League was merely the beginning of a joyous career for Kluivert.

The most important of those seven goals as a teenager was the one that won Ajax the Champions League in 1995, firing in the winner after 85 minutes against a star-studded Milan and becoming the youngest player to score in a Champions League final in the process.

Scoring a first-half hat-trick on his Champions League debut for Red Bull Salzburg against Genk, Haaland looked like a special talent from that very first game and is yet to slow down.

He scored 10 goals as a teenager in the competition and bagged 12 goals in a single Champions League campaign as Manchester City won the trophy for the first time to complete the treble in 2022-23.

Mbappe became the second-youngest Frenchman to score in the Champions League behind Benzema when he fired in a goal in Monaco’s 5-3 defeat away to Manchester City in 2017.

That was the first of many and the beginning of an ascent to individual brilliance at PSG

The forward scored five and assisted six from 10 games as PSG made the final in 2020, before losing to Bayern Munich.

He’s not slowed down in Madrid either, although Mbappe is yet to win the Champions League.

Yamal has surpassed Mbappe to become the highest-scoring teenager in Champions League history, breaking the record in November 2026.

The 19-year-old set the new record after scoring in Barcelona’s 3-0 victory over Aston Villa, his 14th career goal in the Champions League.

Considering he doesn’t turn 20 until next summer, there is plenty of time for Yamal to stretch his lead even further.

Michael Carrick singles out Man Utd ‘mischief’ maker for praise after Roma comeback

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Michael Carrick hailed the contribution of Manchester United youngster Shea Lacey in last night’s dramatic comeback win against Roma.

The Red Devils looked destined for another damaging defeat when they deservedly trailed 2-0 at the break, but produced a spirited revival that culminated in Matheus Cunha’s dramatic, late matchwinning brace.

Bruno Fernandes’ twice-taken penalty had already halved the deficit by the time Lacey was introduced at the expense of Amad Diallo with 15 minutes remaining and the teenager played a starring role in the closing stages.

The 19-year-old gave Roma’s left-back a torrid time before producing a sublime assist from which Cunha struck the decisive goal with just four minutes remaining.

Lacey has been used sparingly by Carrick in recent weeks after he impressed during pre-season but the under-fire United boss hinted he is ready to hand the fleet-footed winger more chances after last night’s cameo.

He said: ‘He’s got great ability. That glint in his eyes and he plays with a bit of mischief. He was desperate to do well.

‘I was just as happy seeing him defend as a right back! He gave everyone a lift coming on the pitch. I am so happy.’

Democrats win US House to secure control of Congress in midterms

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Democrats will take control of House committees in January 2027, giving them power to investigate President Donald Trump’s administration.

Democrats have won control of both chambers of the US Congress in the midterm elections, giving the party greater influence over legislation and oversight of President Donald Trump’s administration.

The party won House seats in districts where Trump beat his Democratic opponent by as much as 18 percentage points in 2024. Democrats also flipped at least seven Senate seats in Kansas, Iowa, Texas, Ohio, Maine, North Carolina and South Carolina, according to the Associated Press.

The result could reshape the outlook for crypto legislation and bring greater congressional scrutiny of Trump-linked digital asset businesses. Democratic committee leaders will gain more influence over which bills advance and which industry activities face investigation.

Democrats will take control of House committees in January 2027, allowing them to issue subpoenas and investigate the Trump administration, the president’s family and their business interests, according to NBC News.

Financial markets commentary publication The Kobeissi Letter pointed to affordability as the defining election issue, citing high energy costs and renewed interest rate increases.

Gokhshtein Media founder David Gokhshtein blamed Republican candidates for failing to connect with voters.

“Well, Republicans got their asses handed to them. I thought they’d do better. I was wrong,” he wrote on X Wednesday.

Prediction markets had favored a Democratic sweep. Traders on Kalshi and Polymarket put the probability of Democrats winning both chambers at roughly 75% on Tuesday, before the results were announced.

Sheffield United want to hand 22-year-old a new contract

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Sheffield United want to hand the midfielder an extension at Bramall Lane in the near future. He is currently tied down with SUFC until the summer of 2028.

Peck, 22, is an important player for the Blades and has enjoyed plenty of game time under Chris Wilder over recent times. He has made 16 appearances for the South Yorkshire outfit in all competitions this season, 14 of which have come in the Championship, and he has chipped in with four goals and three assists.

In this latest update regarding his long-term situation, The Star claim his current club are eager to reward him with fresh terms in an attempt to fend off future interest in his services. He scored a hat-trick for Wilder’s side in their 3-2 away win at Preston North End at Deepdale on Tuesday night.

Sheffield United’s victory over the Lilywhites in Lancashire has seen them rise up to 9th in the table after 14 fixtures and they are only outside the play-offs on goal difference behind West Ham in 8th. They are back in action this weekend away at struggling Charlton Athletic.

Sheffield United will be pleased with Peck’s development. SUFC could face a battle to keep hold of him down the line though amid expected attention from elsewhere.

The England youth international is still young and has potential to grow and develop further in the long run. He battles it out with the likes of Kalvin Phillips, Joe Rothwell and Oli Arblaster, among others, for a place in Wilder’s starting XI.

Peck, who is from Worthing, joined the Blades back in 2021 and has since established himself as one of their most prized assets. He was loaned out back in 2022 to Oldham Athletic in the National League to get some experience under his belt and to boost his development.

He has since broken into the first-team and has made over 100 outings now. Prior to his move to Sheffield United, he rose up through the academy of Premier League giants Arsenal and was a regular for the Gunners at various different youth levels before heading up north.

Sheffield United could do with sorting Peck’s situation as soon as they can. SUFC risk losing him for free in less than two years and they can’t afford for that to happen.

West Ham were linked with a swoop for him in the last transfer window. It remains to be seen whether the Hammers are still interested in him ahead of January.

The Blades are going under the radar a bit at the moment. They have the chance to keep their momentum going with another victory on Saturday at Charlton Athletic.

Ipswich Town star warned of Birmingham City transfer ‘regret’ - Middlesbrough must pounce instead

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Birmingham City and Middlesbrough are two of the Championship's most ambitious clubs at present, and due to their strong starts to the season, there is every chance they could meet in next season's Premier League.

It has been almost a decade since the Reds were last testing their metal against English football's other 19 elite sides, whilst for those supporting Blues, you have to go back to 2011 to remember such heady days.

Kim Hellberg's men were agonisingly close to realising such a dream at the end of last season too, before falling short in the automatic promotion race and then losing the play-off final to Hull City in dramatic circumstances.

Steve Gibson has since backed the highly-rated Swede to complete the promotion mission in his first full season at the Riverside Stadium, and so far, the signs are extremely positive with just one defeat in their first 13 league matches.

The quality of Middlesbrough's starting XI has, unquestionably, improved since the end of 2025/26, with Will Lanskhear's goalscoring exploits epitomising such a change in fortunes.

However, it must be said that there are still reservations surrounding the overall strength-in-depth in creative areas, and taking advantage of where Birmingham previously fell short could be the tonic.

In recent Championship seasons, Anis Mehmeti's upward trajectory has been well-documented, with the Albanian key to Bristol City's play-off finish in 2023/24, before a continuation of such stunning form led to a move to Ipswich Town in January.

The Tractor Boys' ability to secure his signature for just £3.5m raised eyebrows, and so has his lack of involvement at Portman Road despite the club's Premier League return and a change of head coach, with Gary O'Neil replacing Kieran McKenna.

Mehmeti has only played 20 times for Town since his move, with his solitary appearance this season coming in the EFL Cup defeat to Arsenal, in which he scored.

Beforehand, the playmaker was heavily linked with moves to Birmingham, Derby County and Wrexham, before opting to remain in Suffolk - a decision which Football League World's Birmingham City fan pundit, Jason Moore, believes was the wrong one.

Ipswich Town's Anis Mehmeti hasn't featured in a Premier League squad this season after turning down a move to Birmingham City in the summer.

“I think it was a silly decision from Mehmeti and he probably is regretting it. He’s probably miserable because he’s not getting a look-in, as he has wasted six months of his career," he said.

Throughout the summer window, Birmingham were linked with a plethora of attacking midfielders, ranging from Mehmeti, Adrian Segecic of Portsmouth and Joe Gelhardt, who eventually sealed a permanent return to Hull City in August.

It was seen as a major problem that Davies hadn't struck an agreement for a '10' in the summer, given Jay Stansfield's prior struggles in said position.

However, the record signing in the West Midlands has since looked far more assured in the role and is having a strong impact on attacking performances at St. Andrew's as a result, even if his output hasn't aligned with such strong performance levels.

As such, it has begged the question as to whether swooping for Mehmeti's services in January is the right thing for Birmingham to do, and if not, Middlesbrough should look to front the queue.

As well as featuring in a central role, the nine-time international has also caused plenty of havoc when featuring on the left flank, which would provide a strong rotation option with the duo of Tommy Conway and Riley McGree, who have posted seven assists between them so far this season.

Mehmeti's energy levels and versatility also make him a viable option given Hellberg's relentless style of play at the Riverside Stadium, which Leo Castledine hasn't been able to adapt to, for many reasons, since his transfer 10 months ago.

Having set League One alight on loan at Huddersfield Town, there were high hopes for the 21-year-old, who has since suffered injury misfortune and has only played 18 times for Middlesbrough thus far.

Manchester United: Michael Carrick reveals half-time team talk that may have saved his job

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Michael Carrick has detailed the half-time team talk which inspired Manchester United’s come-from-behind win over Roma, and could save his job.

An extraordinary Champions League night at Old Trafford had started grimly, featuring renewed fan demonstrations directed at owners the Glazer family alongside a feeble first-half performance that the visitors ruthlessly punished.

Matias Soule seized on an avoidable opener before Donyell Malen doubled the lead from the spot, prompting loud boos at the interval, before Carrick’s side dug deep to pull off a memorable turnaround.

Captain Bruno Fernandes converted a retaken penalty prior to Matheus Cunha scoring a dramatic late double – and receiving an even later red card – as the Red Devils halted their nine-game winless run in style.

"[’I’m] proud of a lot of people, actually," United head coach Carrick said. "Proud of the players. I think they deserve a lot of credit for pulling through that in a tough situation.

"And for the supporters, you know, to be there. I could feel they were desperate for us to give them something to get their teeth into.

"Frustrating, disappointing, as we all were going in at half-time.

“But I spoke with the players about the second half, about what this place can do and moments, and one moment can really flip it.

"We came out second half really well, just stayed really positive through the whole second half.

"To come through and see so many positives and had to keep going to get that at the end, I’m really pleased for an awful lot of people for that one.

"It’s one win, so we’re not getting too carried away with things, but it was it was an important one for us."

He added: “It’s important for some of the players to have the feeling that they maybe haven’t had here before.

“Some of the lads that came this year, some of the boys that came even last year who maybe haven’t had a feeling of that.

Gold, silver and precious metals prices jump, dollar dips as Democrats take control of Congress

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Precious metals saw strong gains on Tuesday morning, while the value of the dollar fell, following the U.S. midterm elections.

Spot gold was 2.64% higher at $4,031.98 an ounce by 3:37 a.m. ET, while spot silver jumped 4.17% to trade at $51.8495 per ounce.

Front-month gold futures added 2.53% to trade at $4,039.90, while silver futures were up 4.21% at $51.85.

Other metals seeing notable gains on Wednesday morning included platinum, which jumped 2.82% to trade at $1,455.96, and palladium, which was 3.8% higher at $969.33.

The moves came as vote counting continued in the midterms, with the Democrats projected to have won control of the House and multiple governors' offices also turning blue.

U.S. stock futures broadly fell as the results came in, while Treasury yields also moved lower. Oil prices were marginally higher, while the U.S. dollar index shed around 0.3%.

Rhona O'Connell, head of market analysis for EMEA and Asia at StoneX, said in a note on Tuesday that if the Democrats won dual Congressional control, gold should find support as rates and the dollar would likely come under pressure.

She said it would result in "fiscal brinkmanship, institutional friction, both leading to uncertainty," adding that "the Iran war and a weaker dollar would compete with [the] impact of [a] hawkish Fed."

"Less fiscal stimulus argues for lower long yields and a softer dollar, offset by debt-limit premia in bills and a Fed leaning towards another hike," she said.

Both gold and silver enjoyed record-breaking rallies in 2025, surging 66% and 135% respectively over the course of the year as investors sought safe haven assets amid geopolitical uncertainty and broader market volatility.

But the momentum has come undone this year, with rising inflation, surging bond yields and hawkish central banks removing support from the metals. Gold is down more than 7% year-to-date, while silver has tumbled almost 28%.

Spot gold rose on Monday as oil prices retreated, and is now on course for a weekly rise of around 3.7%.

Daniela Hathorn, senior market analyst at Capital.com, said in a Tuesday note that the recovery remains fragile.

"The challenge for precious metals is that geopolitical uncertainty is not automatically translating into safe-haven demand when investors are also concerned about restrictive monetary policy," she said. "They also face the risk of liquidation during sharp equity selloffs, as investors sell profitable or liquid positions to raise cash."

Why Is The Crypto Market Going Up Today?

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Bitcoin is climbing on Wednesday as the end of the US midterm elections removes a major source of uncertainty. BTC gained 1.8% to about $77,920 in early US hours, bouncing back from its lowest level in more than a month, hit in the previous session. During the vote count, the price briefly slid toward $75,700 before recovering to around $77,600. Traders stepped in to buy after weeks of steep losses, helped by US stock futures and Asian markets recovering from early declines, especially in tech. Altcoins edged up as well, with Cardano rising 1.4% and Solana, BNB and Dogecoin each gaining about 0.8%. History also offers support, since Bitcoin has averaged a 54% gain in the 12 months after each of the three previous midterms. Risks remain. A Democratic-controlled Congress could make crypto regulation tougher, as most pro-crypto laws of the past two years had Republican backing.

Manchester United midfielder Bruno Fernandes is reportedly no longer a top target for Saudi Pro League club Al Hilal as he also slams “lies” over his relationship with manager Michael Carrick.

It’s been a difficult season for Fernandes and everyone at Old Trafford, with the Portugal international also recently dropped after hitting out at the tactics of Carrick and his coaching team in the defeat against Como.

United were battered 4-1 by the Serie A side, prompting Fernandes to question the way the team was set up, with Carrick then benching him for the next game against Bournemouth.

Still, it seems Al Hilal are not pursuing Fernandes, according to talkSPORT, while he’s also been quoted as hitting out at the media coverage he’s received.

“It’s been tough, there’s been a lot of speculation in the media,” Fernandes told Amazon Prime, as quoted by BBC Sport.

“A lot of lies around many things that have happened between me and the manager, that we have cleared the air, then after that the manager took the decision to put me on the bench because I’m not performing. That’s fair.

“Everyone thought that was a punishment for the words that I had. And, if it was, it was right. The manager is the one who makes the decisions.

“It doesn’t change the belief I have of the manager and the staff and every player in this club. It doesn’t change my aim to win every game that I have ahead of me.”

Still, it’s unclear what the long-term future holds for Fernandes as talks over a new contract have stalled, according to talkSPORT.

MUFC have the option to extend his contract by one more year, but it could also be good to get a proper commitment from the 32-year-old.

If he continues not to renew with the club then it’s surely a signal that he’ll be open to leaving, even if Al Hilal no longer looks like an option for him.

‘Painful’ – Alexis Mac Allister doesn’t hold back about ‘unpleasant situation’ at Liverpool

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Alexis Mac Allister has spoken out about his ‘painful, unpleasant situation’ at Liverpool in terms of not being offered a new contract.

The Argentine caused a stir in September when he said that he was ‘very sad‘ not to have been offered a new deal by the club, having seen fellow 2023 signings Ryan Gravenberch and Dominik Szoboszlai both pen contract extensions earlier this year.

The Telegraph’s Dominic King reported last month that Anfield chiefs aren’t in ‘any rush’ to begin talks over a new deal for the Reds’ number 10 (who’s contracted to June 2028), despite his resurgence in form since the start of this season.

Appearing on the Los Edul show in his homeland, Mac Allister spoke candidly about Liverpool’s decision not to offer him new terms over the past few months.

The 27-year-old said: “We’re in a situation where I have a year and a half left on my contract. Last season I was told that the club’s idea was not to renew it, at least for now, and obviously that was a painful, unpleasant situation.”

When asked if the situation was making him despondent, he replied: “Not that much, because at the end of the day, I still had – and still have – time left at this club, which is what I want most – to stay here.

“The reality is that the manager has now changed, the sporting director has changed, and new investors are coming into the club, so I think I’m now in a position where anything can happen. We’ll see what happens in the future. I’ve always said that I’m very happy here, that I really enjoy it, and we’ll see.”

Mac Allister added: “Look at Szoboszlai and Gravenberch. They’re two players who were in the same contractual situation as me, and when I was told that, they renewed their contracts.

“It was more a position taken by the club, and generally that’s how things are done. At the end of the day, as a club you don’t want to lose a lot of money, and you don’t want players leaving on free transfers either.”

That the Argentine has spoken out publicly about his contract situation once more gives a strong hint that privately he’s seething at not being offered a new deal in the same way that Szoboszlai and Gravenberch were.

Admittedly Mac Allister’s collapse in form last season may have triggered a reluctance from the club to commit to a contract extension for a player who’ll be 29 when his current terms expire, and he’s a bit older than his aforementioned midfield teammates.

However, the ex-Brighton man has been excellent in the first two months of this campaign, almost as though he’s motivated by a burning desire to earn a new deal at Anfield and show that the hierarchy have been mistaken in their reluctance to tie him down for the long-term.

The 2022 World Cup winner is currently on £180,000 per week at Liverpool, a figure exceeded by 10 of his teammates (Capology), and it could be the case that he’s seeking a higher amount than what the club are prepared to offer him.

Mac Allister could hardly have made his desire for a new contract any clearer. We don’t know exactly why his situation is taking longer to resolve than those of Szoboszlai and Gravenberch, but if he keeps performing on the pitch, then surely it’s just a matter of when and not if he’s offered an extension by the Anfield hierarchy.

Manchester United in agreement over Michael Carrick sack decision after Roma win

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Michael Carrick has eased pressure on his job with Manchester United ‘s come-from-behind win over Roma, but the threat of the sack remains alive.

The under-pressure manager ended a run of three straight defeats - and nine games without a win - as Matheus Cunha scored twice, and was sent off, late on to down an under-strength Roma side.

Roma had raced into a 2-0 lead inside 17 minutes thanks an early Matias Soule strike and a Donyell Malen penalty. But Bruno Fernandes halved the deficit shortly after half-time before Cunha’s late double.

Carrick will now hope his team have momentum ahead of a crucial clash with Aston Villa on Sunday before the international break. The head coach will now that he is not yet out of the woods.

United's hierarchy held crisis talks over the head coach's position after a 3-0 defeat home defeat to the hands Bournemouth earlier in October.

Chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox were involved in discussions at Carrington the following days, with replacements reportedly considered.

Aston Villa boss Unai Emery, former Newcastle manager Eddie Howe and Julian Nagelsmann, fresh from leaving Germany, are among the names who continue to be linked with the vacancy. Antonio Conte has also been mentioned as a possible option.

However, United opted against making an immediate change last month, allowing Carrick to remain in charge for defeat at Chelsea and Tuesday’s late triumph over Roma.

According to The Athletic, Man Utd chiefs last week agreed to keep Carrick in post for the visit of Roma and Sunday’s visit of Aston Villa in the Premier League before reassessing the situation during the break.

That stance is not expected to change despite a much-needed victory.

Man Utd chiefs are said to be happy for Carrick to remain in charge until the international break, at which point a new manager could be appointment and given extra time to settle in the job before domestic football return.

Liverpool are reportedly interested in signing an exciting playmaker who could replace Florian Wirtz.

A rebuild is always going to have teething problems. That's what Liverpool are going through at the moment. There are numerous little areas of concern in the squad that the club's new sporting director Julian Ward is going to have to address.

Under these circumstances, while balancing an imperfect squad, Andoni Iraola has done a stellar job at Anfield.

The Spaniard has come in, embraced the culture of Liverpool and imbued his tactical style and philosophy very quickly into the core of the Reds.

It's a style that, conversely to Arne Slot's, is entertaining and has made Anfield exciting to attend for fans looking to express their love for the club.

But beyond the first problem, Iraola's biggest task at Anfield now remains solving the Florian Wirtz mystery. The German has been a real conundrum for both Slot and Iraola. Every big action movie streaming this November, ranked by Rotten Tomatoes score

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Skip the scrolling and get to streaming high-octane joy rides

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Whether you are dodging holiday stress or settling in for a cozy evening on the couch, losing yourself in the high-octane spectacle of an action movie is a great way to wile away an hour or two. Thankfully, the best streaming services just added hundreds of movies on the first of November, including a ton of action movies to get your adrenaline pumping.

To help you skip the endless scrolling for what to watch and jump straight to the best stunts, car chases, and gunfights, I've tracked down every major action movie that just hit Netflix, Prime Video, HBO Max, and other major streaming platforms and ranked them from best to worst according to their critics' score on Rotten Tomatoes.

Here is what's truly Certified Fresh — and what you may want to skip. Though who amongst us hasn't indulged in revisiting a bad movie from time to time?

Directed by Rian Johnson and with some incredible performances from Joseph Gordon-Levitt and Bruce Willis, "Looper" is a clever sci-fi thriller that combines time travel, killer action sequences and moral quandaries into one thrilling masterpiece. It's a standout in a genre with more than its fair share of duds, a thought-provoking take on a familiar concept that doesn't skimp on the high-octane set pieces.

"Looper" takes us forward to 2074, imagining a world where time travel is invented, but made illegal, and quickly taken up by the mob, who send their targets back in time to be taken out by a "Looper" (aka hitman). Gordon-Levitt's Joe is riding high atop the Looper ranks when his older self, played by Bruce Willis, comes to cut his career short — setting up an epic battle where the past and future collide.

I watched "Black Bag" after my colleague Malcolm described it as a cross between "Ocean’s Eleven" and "Mr. and Mrs. Smith," and I couldn’t agree more. Tightly crafted, smart, and with plenty of dark humor to spare, the film stars Michael Fassbender and Cate Blanchett as a married couple who are both spies for the British intelligence service.

Sign up to the weekly Tom's Streaming Guide to stay in the loop with the latest releases and top recommendations across your favorite streaming services. We’ve got you covered with a complete guide on what’s worth watching.

Fassbender’s George is a human lie detector, but when his wife's suspected of being a potential double agent, he’s forced to use his skills on the one person he thought he knew best. "Black Bag" is a sleek and satisfying thriller, and clocking in at just 94 minutes, it nails that ever-escalating sense of tension you're looking for in an action movie without overstaying its welcome.

If you never got around to checking out the rebooted "Planet of the Apes" franchise, all four movies are now on HBO Max this month. As far as reboots go, it's among the stronger ones, with CGI that holds up surprisingly well even now. The first entry, "Rise of the Planet of the Apes" follows Will Rodman (James Franco), who is testing a cure for Alzheimer's disease on chimpanzees, which dramatically increases their intelligence.

One of these chimpanzees, named Caesar (portrayed by Andy Serkis of "Lord of the Rings" fame), becomes smart enough to recognize the injustices faced by his kind. After being placed in an abusive primate shelter, Caesar leads a rebellion of genetically enhanced apes, sparking a war between humans and apes that doesn't go too well for us. If you don't mind jumping ahead (each story is pretty self-contained), 2024's "Kingdom of the Planet of the Apes" is the latest entry.

Watch "Rise of the Planet of the Apes" on HBO Max now (along with its three sequels)

Vietnam War movies are a dime a dozen, but very few can boast a perfect 100% critics' score on Rotten Tomatoes. "Hamburger Hill" can, and once you watch it, you'll understand why. Set during the last stages of the Vietnam War, "Hamburger Hill" tells the story of a unit of the Army’s 101st Airborne Division led by Lt. Frantz (Dylan McDermott) that, in May 1969, spent 10 days trying to take a hill held by North Vietnamese soldiers.

Five fresh-faced recruits, Beletsky (Tim Quill), Languilli (Anthony Barrile), Washburn (Don Cheadle), Bienstock (Tommy Swerdlow), and Galvan (M.A. Nickles), join war-weary soldiers in an uphill battle that epitomizes the visceral horrors that defined the Vietnam War. They ultimately managed to win the day, but in a pyrrhic victory that's still considered to be a military disaster by most historians.

“Wargames” is a sci-fi techno-thriller that's only gotten more relevant over the years. A sleeper hit when it debuted in the '80s, it became a cult classic and even influenced real-world discussions on cybersecurity. It was also one of Hollywood's best early depictions of hacking that was both thrilling and eerily plausible.

Matthew Broderick stars as David Lightman, a teenage computer whiz who accidentally hacks into a top-secret U.S. military supercomputer, thinking Global Thermonuclear War sounds like a fun new computer game. He runs a simulation of what a nuclear war between the U.S. and the Soviet Union would look like — one that the computer, known as WOPR, begins to take seriously.

Could Buying This 1 Cryptocurrency Make You Rich?

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Venice Token has skyrocketed in value this year. But can it continue its momentum over the long haul?

For the year, Venice Token (VVV +17.41%) is up a heart-pounding 1,250%. That makes it the top-performing cryptocurrency with a market cap of $1 billion or higher.

For good reason, then, some investors have embraced Venice Token as a potential millionaire-maker and a pathway to life-changing wealth. But is that really the case?

Artificial intelligence continues to be one of the few bright spots in the crypto market this year. Traditional market leaders Bitcoin and Ethereum are down 12% and 22%, respectively, for the year. Meanwhile, AI cryptos continue to soar much higher.

Of all the top AI crypto stars, Venice Token has burned the brightest, with its 1,250% return. But don't forget about Bittensor or NEAR Protocol, the latter of which is up 133% for the year. Both also represent ways to get exposure to AI via blockchain technology.

But investors have latched on to Venice Token specifically because it powers a privacy-focused version of ChatGPT. In other words, you can type in a quick prompt on the Venice AI platform and get a response in the form of text, images, or video. Best of all, your usage of the platform remains anonymous and private, so you never have to worry about large language models training on your personal data in the future.

But here's the thing: You can't simultaneously believe in companies such as Anthropic or OpenAI and Venice at the same time. Their vision of AI is very different. Anthropic and OpenAI are pushing a vision of centralized AI, in which the majority of power and control lies within the corporation. Venice is pushing a vision of decentralized, privacy-focused AI built with blockchain technology.

And that's where things get tricky for investors. The thinking until recently has been that the upcoming Anthropic and OpenAI IPOs will lift all AI boats. And that includes AI cryptocurrencies such as Venice Token.

According to this way of thinking, even more money will flood into the AI crypto space after the IPOs, helping to push up the price of Venice Token. That means crypto investors might be able to squeeze another year of triple- or even quadruple-digit returns out of Venice.

But I don't think so. The AI crypto space is littered with big names that soar in value for one year before ultimately collapsing. Several prominent early AI crypto names have already fallen by the wayside. Even AI cryptos specifically pegged to the AI agent investment thesis have already imploded.

So what makes investors think Venice Token will be any different? It's still too early to pick winners and losers in the AI space. That makes it problematic to pick cryptocurrencies you can buy and hold for the long haul. Venice makes for an interesting short-term momentum play, but is simply too risky and volatile as a long-term wealth creation tool.

Dominic Basulto has positions in Bitcoin and Ethereum. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Bitcoin, Bittensor, Ethereum, NEAR Protocol, and Venice Token. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Zcash Is Getting a Major Upgrade in November. Could This Be a Catalyst for Further ZEC Price Gains?

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The red-hot cryptocurrency has a significant upgrade just days away.

Zcash (ZEC +14.00%), the largest privacy coin by market cap, is about to get a major technical upgrade around Nov. 5. The NU7 network upgrade will cut the time between blocks of transactions from 75 seconds to 25, making confirmations roughly three times faster.

Despite a rough year for the crypto market, Zcash has exploded in value, gaining 134% year to date. Let's take a closer look at the NU7 upgrade to see what it changes and whether it could drive further growth.

The main headline of the NU7 upgrade is the change in block time. Like other cryptocurrencies, Zcash records transactions on a blockchain, with each block containing groups of transactions. Blocks currently have a target confirmation time of 75 seconds, which will drop to 25 seconds after the upgrade.

This doesn't mean transactions go through in 25 seconds. Each exchange sets a required number of confirmations to fully verify a transaction, based on its risk tolerance. If an exchange waits for three confirmations, then transaction times on its platform would drop from nearly four minutes (225 seconds) to 75 seconds.

NU7 also changes Zcash's fee allocation. Starting at activation, 60% of each block's transaction fees are removed from circulation and placed in the network sustainability mechanism, a reserve that provides a security budget for the network. Miners keep the other 40% of transaction fees. Originally, 100% of block fees went to miners. Like Bitcoin, Zcash has a hard cap of 21 million coins, so the reserve prepares for a day when fees pay miners rather than newly issued coins.

In addition, the upgrade adds per-block limits on shielded transactions and shuts down Sprout, the blockchain's original privacy pool. Funds must be transferred out of Sprout before activation, or they'll become unusable.

Execution issues are the primary risk for the NU7 upgrade, especially since it's being activated on a faster schedule than usual. Zcash's rules normally call for activation to occur approximately three months after release of the first software for the new network. This provides exchanges, node operators, and miners with ample time to update their software before it expires.

NU7's deployment document waived the three-month requirement. A release candidate started running on Zcash's test network early last month, but the stable software for the main network only launched on Oct. 22. Anyone using outdated software could end up on a different chain.

The closure of the Sprout pool also presents potential issues. Any funds left there will be lost forever, and moving funds off that pool could temporarily expose transaction amounts.

NU7 is an important upgrade to the Zcash network, both shortening block times and setting aside fee revenue in a reserve. But I wouldn't expect it to have a big impact on the privacy coin's price.

Blockchain upgrades aren't surprises. They're scheduled in advance, giving traders time to buy in and often turning the actual activation dates into "sell-the-news" events. Zcash has also already been one of the most successful crypto investments this year, so an upgrade may not be enough to move the needle at this point.

For investors, it's still worth watching how activation goes. A clean switch to NU7 would be a positive sign, while a delay or glitch could weigh on sentiment. Either way, the upgrade's value is more likely to show up in how Zcash works than in its price.

Lyle Daly has positions in Bitcoin. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Bitcoin. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

PSG: Mika Godts’ time has finally arrived!

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

With the injuries to Ousmane Dembélé and Khvicha Kvaratskhelia, all eyes are naturally turning to Désiré Doué, expected to take more responsibility in the Parisian attack. But another player also had a good card to play: Mika Godts. Recruited this summer to replace, on paper, Bradley Barcola, the Belgian winger has so far struggled to convince. His entries into the game had often left a taste of unfinished business, to the point that the possibility of an offensive reinforcement was already mentioned to compensate for the absence of Kvaratskhelia. As if we still doubted the ability of his recruit to meet expectations. Facing Olympique Lyonnais this Monday evening, Godts therefore had an ideal opportunity to silence the first criticisms.

Still replacing at kick-off, the former Ajax player was launched while PSG came up against a particularly compact Lyon block. His first minutes, however, long resembled his previous appearances. Available, multiplying calls in depth, the Belgian was rarely served by his partners. But he never gave up, displaying constant involvement, including in defensive tasks. He notably stood out with a magnificent save on his line to prevent OL from scoring. The fact remains that Godts was expected offensively. And this time, he responded. First a decisive passer for Senny Mayulu on the opening score in Paris, he then scored his first goal in the colors of PSG with a clear right-footed strike which left Dominik Greif no chance.

A liberating performance for the Belgian winger, who did not hide his satisfaction in the mixed zone after the match. “It’s good to have scored and helped the team. I just wanted to help the team. What if I was waiting for this goal? Even before that, I am calm. I try to work every day. And when the coach needs me, I’m there to help the team. I’m happy, when you’re a striker you want to score and help with goals.”he explained. Happy to have finally unlocked his score in his new club, Godts also wanted to emphasize the importance of those around him in his adaptation. “It’s good to score here, I’m happy. I feel good in the team, they help me on and off the pitch. When I scored, I thought of my family, my wife, they are important to me and it is thanks to them that I can work hard. »

For his part, Senny Mayulu did not fail to praise the performance of his teammate, with whom he combined perfectly to open the score. “I’m happy for him, we have a good connection. But it’s with everyone, we’re a family. So I’m happy that Godts passed me and scored, it’ll do him good.”confided the young Parisian midfielder in the mixed zone. An evening which could mark a turning point in Mika Godts’ Parisian adventure. Until now struggling to find his place in the offensive rotation, the Belgian finally showed what he could bring to Luis Enrique’s collective. While PSG must deal with several major absences in attack, this performance comes at the best time. It is now up to him to confirm to prove that he can become a real offensive solution for the coming weeks.

Gym-goers, Fitness Plus just got a hell of a lot better!

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I’ve been writing about Apple Fitness Plus since it launched in December 2020 — what started as workouts with your favorite trainers has evolved into a much larger ecosystem, and this might be one of its most exciting transformations to date. Today, Apple has announced new Gym Strength plans, launching on Fitness Plus.

These personalized four-week training plans move away from group classes and more towards helping users reach their own fitness goals. The plans are progressive and work with larger commercial gyms, as well as with minimal equipment at home. Let’s dive into everything you can expect from this update:

Gym Strength is designed to take the guesswork out of the gym. You’ll find it in the Fitness Plus menu, and you’ll be asked to answer a few questions before you begin. This helps the algorithm to create a programme best suited to you. You’ll input how familiar you are with strength training, your goal, the areas of your body you want to work, the days of the week you prefer to work out on, and, most importantly, the equipment you have available.

Once you’ve got your plan, you can turn on Apple Intelligence-powered training notes, or leave them off. The note is designed to summarize your progress and give you an overview of how this workout can help you reach your goals.

One of the most difficult things about working out alone is making sure you’re moving with good form. You can move through the Gym Strength workouts at your own pace, with your own music, but each exercise will be demoed by a Fitness Plus trainer to ensure you’re getting the form right. You can tap on the video to expand it and see the trainer from three different angles.

Each exercise has recommended sets, reps, and load, depending on what you’ve lifted before and what you are hoping to achieve. You can easily tap Swap on an exercise if you just don’t want to do it, and swap out the equipment available if you’re in a busy gym and don’t want to wait.

On-screen, you’ll also see a dynamic bar, which shows you at a glance how you’re progressing through your workout.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

Of course, this is Apple, so seamless integration is a given. You can see your exercises, reps, and load from your wrist if you’re using an Apple Watch during the workout, as well as what’s coming next.

You can also see your heart rate data and how many calories you’ve burned.

Unlike a strength plan you’ve downloaded from the internet or grabbed from ChatGPT, the Gym Strength plans are designed to progress with you session by session. With input from the Fitness Plus strength trainers, the plans give you an estimated load when suggesting an exercise you’ve not done before. Using your inputs, the plan will tell you when it thinks you need to increase the weight you’re lifting, based on your past performances.

I’ll be grabbing some weights and heading to the gym to test my own Gym Strength plan over the next few weeks. Watch this space!

Trumper’s Remorse: Voters Look at the Last Two Years, Ask for a Redo

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

With crushing wins in the House and Senate, America wants to Replayola everything that happened since 2024.

Even the most forgiving retailers tend to draw a line on a return policy of 90 days. Show up after that, you’re out of luck. But voters across America on Tuesday took a look at what they purchased two Novembers ago and said: “How about two years; would you accept that? The merchandise is a little battered, but the tags are intact.”After months of political fighting both substantive and surreal, with debates over ideology and reflections on buttocks, American voters Tuesday delivered as strong a rebuff to a Republican president as has been delivered in four decades, turning the House of Representatives and Senate over to Democrats and essentially saying “you see if you can stop him.”Democrats won in swing states and they won in red states; they won in states where Trump had been ascendant and states where Republicans never gave up control. And they won, improbably, in rural counties all over the country.From Maine to Texas, Kansas to South Carolina — hell, as of this writing even Mississippi was in play — new Democratic Senators sprung up everywhere. They won so often cable news pundits ran out of water puns for their blue-wave metaphor.Democrats gained, as of this writing, a whopping 41 seats in the House, far more than the three they needed to win control. But it was the Senate — where a flip was far from assured and in fact unlikely just weeks ago — where the most drama abided.A need for four flips was filled, then it turned into five, then six. States where Democrats hadn’t won a Senate seat in 38 years (Texas) or in 94 (Kansas). States where Trump two years ago won by double digits (Ohio) and states where Strom Thurmond presided for half a century (South Carolina).And, maybe most unlikely, states where Democrats hadn’t beaten a Susan Collins in 30 years (Maine).As of this writing, Democrats had flipped seven Senate seats with an eye on an eighth in Alaska and a ninth in Mississippi. They were also holding on to all of the blue seats, including Michigan, called by CNN for Abdul El-Sayed at 2:30 am local time.There have been major Congressional shifts like his before, most notably in 1994 for Republicans and 2008 for Democrats. But both of those events occurred because of a galvanizing candidate in the winning party — Newt Gingrich in 1994 and Barack Obama in 2008. What was remarkable about this sweep is that it didn’t come from one central charismatic figure but a slew of grassroots personalities who understood their individual constituencies, state-by-state: James Talarico and Josh Turek and Annie Andrews and Adam Hamilton. (Also, speaking of Talarico and Hamilton, it’s nice Democrats finally figured out what the Republicans did decades ago: overtly Christian candidates play.)And, of course, the Trump remorse. Because there was a political figure who mobilized the electorate — just in the negative. How fittingly 2026 that the candidate who influenced voters most was the one people liked least, Donald Trump with his ICE and Iran and the fact that prices still seem to be going up while the only thing the president is bringing down is the East Wing.Perhaps the best modern comp to all this is 1986. That Election Day 40 years ago, Democrats also scored big in the midterms — also in the second term of a fading Republican icon, netting eight seats against Ronald Reagan.This election season has been one of the most entertaining, if also the most divisive, in recent memory as a showman president disliked even by his own party paved the way to a series of colorful showdowns.Books can be written about all the dynamics, but fights over Middle East policy roiled and at times threatened to break apart the Democratic party — right up until the final days with Haley Stevens’ shocking defection in Michigan. And yet somehow it all led to history — the first openly gay male senator in New Hampshire’s Chris Pappas, the first Black female governor in Keisha Lance Bottoms.Meanwhile, Republicans tried to look toward the future while grappling with the Donald Trump present, accepting his campaign bids even as they kept him out of nearly all of their ads. (Democrats, on the other hand, were all too happy to tether their opponents to the president.)And through it all the president himself, getting distracted with boasts and random talk as he hit the hustings on a campaign trail where he was only ambivalently wanted.The Democrats will face mountains ahead, not least thanks to the schisms in their own party; how will the Darializa Chevalier and Melat Kiros types in the House play with the mainstream types led by new speaker Hakeem Jeffries (the first Black Speaker in American history), and in the Senate how will Peggy Flanagan and Abdul El-Sayed meld with moderates like Turek and Hamilton, not to mention majority leader Chuck Schumer.The Democrats will also have to decide how much to lean into inquiries and investigations, as this is not what voters sent them to Washington for — it’s the economy, stupid, as Bill Clinton’s (and James Carville’s) words continue to redound.But the bigger questions lie with Republicans, who now must decide in the minority how to oppose Democrats — and how much to genuflect to a man they have spent much of the past decade paying fealty to, now newly irrelevant. Perhaps the person most confused today, strategically: JD Vance. What or whom exactly is he supposed to be defending, and how does he find a path to the 2028 primaries intact?All those questions await in January. On this November day Democrats really only care about one: “Would you like that as cash or credit?” Related Stories Midterms 2026 Kimmel Bashes Trump After Democrats' Midterms "Tsunami": "The Only Ass on His Mind Is the One That Just Got Handed to Him" Midterms 2026 Ben Shapiro Says 2026 Midterms Were "A Wipeout for Republicans": "The President Was Kind of on the Ballot"

This is part of The Hollywood Reporter’s ongoing midterm coverage. You can check out all of our pieces here.

Trump Crypto Empire Faces Fresh Heat: Justin Sun Seeks WLFI Sanctions

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Donald Trump crypto empire faces fresh pressure as Republicans lose control of Congress in the 2026 midterms, handing Democrats a sweeping victory. The political setback comes as Trump-linked tokens remain deeply underwater and Justin Sun escalates his legal fight against World Liberty Financial, the WLFI.

Sun is seeking nearly $264,000 in sanctions over alleged failures to disclose the project’s UAE ownership ties, and the midterm defeat gives Democrats greater power to investigate Trump’s administration and scrutinize potential conflicts of interest involving his family’s business interests.

Meanwhile, the market has delivered its own verdict. Official Trump, Melania Meme and WLFI tokens have fallen sharply from their peaks, leaving investors nursing substantial losses.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

The latest legal pressure comes from Justin Sun, who invested $45 million in WLFI tokens before his relationship with the Trump-backed crypto project deteriorated.

Sun sued World Liberty Financial in April, accusing the company of fraud and unlawfully freezing his tokens. The project later countersued, describing his claims as a “meritless PR stunt.”

Now, the Justin Sun dispute has escalated into a fight over WLFI’s corporate ownership disclosures. Sun alleges World Liberty initially identified Florida as its sole citizenship in federal court. Months later, the company amended its filing to identify 22 entities in its ownership chain, claiming citizenship across Florida, Delaware, Puerto Rico, and the United Arab Emirates.

Last night, my lawyers moved to sanction World Liberty Financial @worldlibertyfi for failing to disclose, until months into my litigation against the company, that it is a citizen of the UAE 🇦🇪 . #WLFI #USD1 For those who are new to this dispute, I was one of World Liberty’s…

The amended disclosure came shortly after an August hearing addressing World Liberty’s attempt to move parts of the dispute into arbitration. Sun argues the company should have disclosed its ownership structure earlier, before his legal team spent months challenging arbitration. He claims the late disclosure could undermine the federal court’s jurisdiction.

The WLFI Sanctions motion seeks nearly $264,000 in legal fees and costs from the company and its attorneys. World Liberty’s lawyers reportedly said they learned about the UAE citizenship after the original filing. The court has not determined whether the company intentionally concealed its ownership ties.

Sun plans to refile his lawsuit in California state court once the sanctions motion is resolved. Nevertheless, this controversy adds another complication for Trump Crypto ventures, whose foreign investment connections have already attracted political scrutiny.

The legal dispute arrives as Trump-linked digital assets remain far below their launch-era highs. The official Trump crypto token reached $73.43 in January 2025 before falling to around $1.50. That leaves TRUMP down 98% from its peak.

Melania Meme, the first lady of America-tied meme coin, has suffered an even steeper decline. The token previously reached approximately $13.05 but has since traded near seven cents, representing a loss exceeding 99%.

WLFI has also retreated from its all-time high near $0.33 to roughly five cents. Its fully diluted valuation remains substantial, but its market price reflects a sharp reversal from launch expectations.

The downturn has left Trump crypto investors facing losses while the project’s political and legal connections draw renewed attention.

World Liberty also operates USD1, its dollar-pegged stablecoin, which has grown into a major part of the project’s business. The company’s financial and ownership structure could receive greater scrutiny as Democrats prepare to take control of Congress.

The midterm results have not triggered investigations into Trump crypto ventures, but Democrats will gain subpoena power and greater influence over congressional oversight once the new Congress convenes.

The Trump crypto story is no longer just about political branding and token launches. It is increasingly about investor losses, corporate transparency, and the legal consequences of operating a business tied to the presidency.

Bitcoin bounces from 7-week low under $76K as Democrats sweep Congress

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin price downside reached a seven-week-low of $75,618 as 24-hour crypto long liquidations neared $300 million.

Bitcoin (BTC) sought to erase 3% overnight losses on Wednesday as crypto markets digested the Democratic sweep of the midterm elections.

Data from TradingView showed BTC/USD falling into Tuesday’s daily close as election results came in, with Democrats going on to win control of both the House and Senate. The pair dropped to a low of $75,618 on Bitstamp, a level last seen on Sept. 16, before rebounding as high as $78,039 over the next three hours.

BTC/USD one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Data from CoinGlass showed the price dip liquidating late BTC long positions below $76,000, while a wall of asks kept upside contained below $78,000. Total crypto long liquidations over the past 24 hours stood at $288 million.

BTC liquidation heatmap. Source: CoinGlass

In comments sent to Cointelegraph, Kelly Ye, head of research at institutional digital asset trading platform Talos, said that markets had largely priced in the election outcome.

“Bitcoin moved about 1.7% after the polls closed, close to the roughly 1.8% options had priced, and there was no major leverage flush. The election was the main event, but markets had largely prepared for it,” she said.

US stock futures showed similar moves, initially falling before recouping some losses. S&P 500 and Nasdaq 100 futures were both down by about 0.3% at the time of writing.

S&P 500 futures one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Late Tuesday, Anthony Saglimbene, chief market strategist at financial services company Ameriprise Financial, told CNBC that concerns over data-center and broader AI capital expenditure were producing an adverse market reaction to the Democratic gains.

“If you get some governorships that are Democratic, what does that mean for the data center buildout, which has already seen a lot of skepticism over the last couple weeks in terms of the hyperscaler capex spending?” he asked.

In an interview with The Wall Street Journal, US President Donald Trump, who previously said he could become “Mr. Veto” to block Democratic policy initiatives, struck a conciliatory tone.

“I think we’ll work with the Democrats, and we’ll get along very well with them,” he said.

Bitcoin’s drop to $75,618 coincided with its 50-week simple moving average (SMA), a trend line that has held as support since mid-September.

Related: Here’s what happened in crypto today

In an analysis posted on X, Keith Alan, co-founder of crypto trading suite Material Indicators, identified the 50-week SMA as a line in the sand for Bitcoin bulls. Failure to defend it, he argued, would open the door to a “deeper correction ahead.”

BTC/USD one-day chart with 50 SMA. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Crypto trader and analyst Rekt Capital, who had forecast BTC/USD dipping below $76,000 after a failed breakout attempt above $78,000, continued to flag overhead resistance on the three-day chart.

“If this continues then these newfound 3-Day Lower Highs may guide price back towards the ~$75.5k level below over time,” he said on Wednesday.

BTC/USD three-day chart. Source: Rekt Capital on X.com

Bruno Fernandes admits Michael Carrick was right to drop him in fiery interview

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

United skipper Fernandes was benched last month after criticising Carrick’s tactics but conceded the boss had been right in an interview following the remarkable 3-2 comeback win over Roma

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Bruno Fernandes denied that Manchester United’s remarkable comeback win against Roma saved Michael Carrick from the sack but admitted the manager was right to drop him for criticising his tactics last month.

The United captain scored their first goal with a twice-taken penalty as they came from 2-0 down to beat Roma 3-2 and record their first victory in 10 matches.

But after their previous Champions League game, a 4-1 thrashing by Como, Fernandes had blamed Carrick for United’s lack of pressing in a post-match interview – and the United manager responded by leaving him as an unused substitute for their next match, a 3-0 loss to Bournemouth.

And Fernandes addressed the issue in another fiery post-match interview following the Roma comeback.

Speaking to Amazon Prime Video, he said: “The manager took a decision of making myself be on the bench because I wasn’t performing, and that’s fair.

“Everyone felt that was a punishment for the words that I had said. And if it was, it was right. The manager is the one that makes the decisions. I have to accept them. I have to go forward with that.

“It doesn’t change the belief I have on the manager and on the staff and and every player that is in this club, and it doesn’t change my aim to win every f***ing game that I have ahead of me.”

Fernandes and Carrick subsequently held talks before the Portuguese was brought back into the team on Saturday against Chelsea.

And as he got increasingly passionate, Fernandes added: “It’s been tough. It’s been a lot of speculation in the media, a lot of lies around many things that have happened in this club between me and the manager, like that we had cleared the air then after when nothing of that happened.”

Carrick has been under pressure as United have only taken seven points in the Premier League this season and had lost their three previous matches in all competitions.

But Fernandes said: “We don’t need to save anyone’s job. But it was important. The club needed that win, the players needed that win. It’s something that we needed as a group of players together with the staff.”

From embarrassment to the Man United of old – Michael Carrick saves his job, for now...

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Man United 3-2 Roma: Carrick was on the brink of the sack at 2-0 down inside 20 minutes but United ended their nine-game winless streak in remarkable fashion

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Michael Carrick may still need a greater comeback than this if he is to taste another Champions League night at Old Trafford. But if this proves to be the last, what a way to go. The romantic in Carrick remembers what Manchester United used to be, the days that seemed to be consigned to the past. Suddenly, salvation came from the United of old.

When it seemed Carrick’s job was untenable, there was an extraordinary fightback, a victory plucked from the jaws of defeat, a strange kind of glory from complete ignominy. The crowd who had reacted to the award of a penalty by calling for the Glazer family to go ended up galvanised and roused, cheering a team who had been an absolute embarrassment for 45 minutes to a victory few saw coming, chorusing Carrick’s name.

He has a lifeline. “He needed that win tonight, the club needed that win, the players needed that win,” said the captain Bruno Fernandes. After 54 days without a victory, everyone did. “Big character, big fight,” said Youri Tielemans. “It’s unbelievable, really,” said Kobbie Mainoo.

United were both unbelievably and believably bad at the start. It defied credibility when they emerged as victors.

Carrick had said he knew it sounded crazy when he said he was looking forward to this game. By half-time, his optimism appeared delusional. But now and again, even when they are at their lowest ebb for decades, United can justify the faith.

Carrick had talked on Monday about a magical night against Roma. That was 20 seasons ago, when United destroyed them 7-1. Fast forward from 2007 to 2026 and, out of nowhere, this version of United stumbled across something wondrous of their own. “This place can do magical things,” said Carrick, attributing it to the power of Old Trafford. It was as good an explanation as any.

Roma could be forgiven for wondering what happened, if they are cursed on this ground. Two goals to the good, they almost made it three on the stroke of half-time. Gian Piero Gasperini’s side were showing it was possible to play dynamic attacking football and produce a coherent team in a 3-4-3 formation at Old Trafford, something Ruben Amorim never accomplished despite his best efforts.

Roma’s first win in England in 17 attempts and 25 years appeared a certainty. Instead, they departed defeated, taking regrets for eternity back to the eternal city. Overwhelmed by a Real Madrid fightback in the last 10 minutes two months ago, they suffered again in a strange sequel.

The catalysts for the comeback were Fernandes and Matheus Cunha. The Portuguese scored one goal and made another, showing why Carrick had to restore him to the team after his post-match criticisms of the manager in Italy. The Brazilian had a part in the first goal, scored the equaliser, then the winner and still had time to collect a red card.

Booked for celebrating his goal in the crowd, Cunha was sent off in injury time, sliding recklessly into Leonardo Balerdi. He is out of United’s trip to Lisbon to face Sporting in three weeks’ time.

Victory came at another cost: Carlos Baleba had appeared the first United player to sprint all game when he burst through on goal just after half-time but pulled up holding his hamstring and went off in tears. He presumably will not be seen again until December.

So he went off before the game was transformed. If Carrick has been luckless of late, United may have had twin strokes of fortune. They were awarded a penalty, with Gianluca Mancini deemed to have fouled Cunha, and then a retake after Roma’s third-choice goalkeeper Devis Vazquez saved from Fernandes but Bryan Cristante was punished for encroachment. The United captain’s aim was surer the second time around and he ended his own goal drought, which dated back almost two months.

There was almost an instant equaliser. Cunha released Bryan Mbeumo with a wonderful pass and the Cameroonian’s shot hit both posts but did not cross the line. As Old Trafford rediscovered energy and excitement, Diogo Dalot had a header cleared off the line by Cristante.

Then Cunha doubled his tally of goals for the season within the space of four minutes. When Fernandes crossed, Balerdi inexplicably missed his kick and Cunha slotted in. Shortly after, he whipped in a sumptuous shot off the near post to complete a remarkable turnaround.

“Matheus can do them things,” said Carrick, vaguely. The manager had played his part, too: bringing on Shea Lacey added a threat on the right flank, swapping Cunha and Mbeumo at half-time worked.

And so United’s nine-game run without a win was ended. A second victory in the Champions League this season reduces the chance they will fail to even get a top-24 finish. Yet when the Stretford End threw tennis balls on to the pitch in protest, it was tempting to think that, given United’s problems with football, they were merely seeing if they could fare better with something else.

To begin with, United had surged into a two-goal deficit, conceding twice in the space of eight minutes. It was the earliest any visiting side had ever scored twice at Old Trafford in Europe.

The lack of an alternative left-back has been damaging since Luke Shaw was injured. It was a factor in Roma’s swift double. Preferred to the dropped Lisandro Martinez, Ayden Heaven’s recall swiftly turned hellish. Dalot was dismal, even by his own standards

When Rodrigo Mora swept a cross-field ball and a jumping Heaven only succeeded in heading thin air, Dalot was caught upfield, allowing an unmarked Matias Soule to sweep in a low shot. Remarkably, Dalot was out of position again, allowing Soule room behind him, when the Argentinian’s shot hit Heaven’s arm.

Donyell Malen converted the penalty. It was his 12th goal in 13 games. There was almost a 13th, when Senne Lammens made a fine save after the Red Devils’ defence parted like the Red Sea. Forever unmarked, Soule then curled a shot just wide.

Roma could have finished United, and perhaps Carrick, off. Instead, he has a reprieve. “I don’t have a crystal ball to know what happens next,” he said. But whether with a crystal ball, a tennis ball or a football, this was hard to predict.

Democrats Have Retaken the House and Senate -- but There's an Even Bigger Winner Today

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Congressional Democrats aren’t the only one’s cheering.

Yesterday, tens of millions of Americans made their voices heard by casting or mailing in their ballots. These votes have charted a new course for America, with Democrats taking firm control of the House of Representatives and the Senate, according to the Associated Press. Starting in early January, President Donald Trump will be working with a Democrat-led Congress.

This congressional shake-up is a big win for Democrats. But at the end of the day, there's an even bigger winner: Wall Street's major stock indexes -- the Dow Jones Industrial Average (^DJI -0.38%), S&P 500 (^GSPC +0.36%), and Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC +0.83%) -- and investors.

President Trump will be working with a Democrat-led Congress in early January. Image source: Official White House Photo by Daniel Torok.

President Trump has enjoyed a unified Republican government in the first two years of both of his nonconsecutive terms. In each case, Congress passed, and he signed into law, a major tax and spending bill. The Tax Cuts and Jobs Act (TCJA) (signed December 2017) permanently lowered the peak marginal corporate income tax rate to 21%, fueling S&P 500 share buybacks, while the Big, Beautiful Bill (signed July 2025) made the personal income tax bracket reductions from the TCJA permanent.

A divided Congress likely takes major tax and spending legislation off the table, which isn't necessarily bad news for Wall Street.

But a Democrat-controlled Congress does have some sticking points.

DEMOCRATIC SWEEP? UBS SEES 50% ODDS. UBS assigns a 50% probability to Democrats winning both chambers, while prediction markets put Democratic control of the House at 94% and Senate at 77%. UBS warns a sweep could complicate Trump's fiscal agenda and 2027 debt-ceiling negotiations, while tariffs and AI investment remain largely insulated.

For instance, debt-ceiling negotiations become a lot trickier. The ideological differences between America's two parties increase the likelihood of a lengthy government shutdown that could harm U.S. gross domestic product and corporate earnings.

Democratic lawmakers also favor stricter artificial intelligence (AI) regulations. Whereas the president has minimized AI regulation, Democrats prefer limiting data center expansion and ramping up the testing and vetting of large language models.

There's definitely room for near-term uncertainty to creep into the picture ahead of January 2027.

But if there's one thing history has shown investors, it's that they're the biggest winners on Election Night. No matter how the political puzzle pieces are arranged, the Dow, S&P 500, Nasdaq Composite, and investors always come out as long-term beneficiaries.

Admittedly, certain political combinations work better than others. According to online retirement planning platform Retirement Researcher, the benchmark S&P 500 has, on average, gained 16.63% annually under a Democratic president and a divided Congress since 1926. By comparison, gains taper to an annual average of 7.33% with a Republican president and a split Congress.

However, a 7.33% average annual return still tops other popular asset classes, including Treasury bonds, commodities, and real estate. Even in less-than-ideal historical scenarios, the stock market delivers superior returns.

Furthermore, this data shows that while fiscal policy does play a role in corporate America, it's the operating results, management actions, and innovative prowess of public companies that ultimately push the Dow Jones Industrial Average, S&P 500, and Nasdaq Composite higher over the long term.

The AI infrastructure build-out, the evolution of quantum computing, significant S&P 500 share buybacks, and record-breaking initial public offerings won't stop just because the congressional puzzle pieces will be rearranged in January. With another election in the books, investors are yet again the big winners.

Man Utd 3-2 Roma: Why Michael Carrick Told Matheus Cunha to Leave Pitch

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Rob is senior football editor at GiveMeSport, with over 15 years experience covering sport in various capacities since graduating from the University of Gloucestershire in 2008 with a degree in journalism. Michael Carrick and Matheus Cunha appeared to be involved in a disagreement shortly after Manchester United’s dramatic 3-2 win over AS Roma in the Champions League.

United completed a stunning second-half comeback against their Italian opponents at Old Trafford on Tuesday night, with Cunha playing a starring role.

The Red Devils’ miserable start to the 2026-27 season threatened to continue after conceding two goals inside the opening 17 minutes.

However, a re-taken Bruno Fernandes penalty in the 58th minute turned the game on its head.

Cunha secured Man Utd’s third victory of the campaign with two late goals in the space of three minutes, sending the home crowd wild.

But the Brazilian’s decision to celebrate his second goal by removing his shirt and jumping into the crowd backfired. Spanish referee Jesus Gil Manzano booked Cunha for the celebration, before showing him a second yellow card four minutes later for a late tackle.

Red-carded players are requested to leave the pitch and head back to the dressing room, but Cunha was seen hovering around the tunnel.

When Manzano blew the full-time whistle, Cunha returned to the pitch to celebrate with his teammates.

Manchester United recorded their first win in 10 matches with a much-needed 3-2 comeback success against Roma in the Champions League

TV cameras then spotted an eyebrow-raising incident between Carrick and Cunha, who was visibly unhappy after being instructed to leave the pitch alongside a member of staff, stopping before the tunnel to applaud the fans. Watch the footage below:

Per the Manchester Evening News, it’s understood that Carrick told Cunha to return to the dressing room in order to avoid being hit with another punishment following his dismissal.

However, the South American now faces further punishment after appearing to break the rules - specifically IFAB’s Law 12, covering fouls and misconduct, stating players who are sent off must leave the pitch and head to the dressing room or a spectator area.

An extended ban for Cunha is now a possibility if UEFA decide Cunha is guilty of breaching the laws of the game.

The Brazil international, 27, will already miss United’s next Champions League match against Sporting on November 25. He could also end up missing the clash against RB Leipzig on December 12 if he’s slapped with an additional one-match ban following Tuesday’s antics.

Moreover, Cunha's actions have reportedly left both himself and United at risk of a fine.

“I was a bit disappointed with how it finished [red card] but it just shows how much he cares and [what] he's willing to do when he's in that frame of mind,” Carrick said, per Fabrizio Romano.

Meanwhile, Cunha added: "What a magic day. Of course, apart from the red card.”

He continued: “The mentality was beautiful in the second half. Let it be a turning point in the season."

United’s crucial win over Roma leaves them 17th in the Champions League table, with six points registered from a possible 12.
