Starfish Space loses contact with its Otter spacecraft, ending satellite inspection mission

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

by Alan Boyle on Nov 3, 2026 at 10:50 pmNovember 3, 2026 at 11:10 pm

Starfish Space says it can no longer receive data from its first full-scale Otter space vehicle, bringing a $15 million NASA mission to inspect inoperable satellites to a premature end.

In a LinkedIn post, the Tukwila, Wash.-based startup said its Otter 24C spacecraft was not able to complete all the objectives for the mission, known as Small Spacecraft Propulsion and Inspection Capability (SSPICY). “Starfish and NASA are exploring opportunities to fulfill mission objectives through other Starfish missions,” the company said.

The Otter spacecraft was sent into low Earth orbit aboard a SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket on Oct. 1. “Following a successful launch and deployment, Starfish established two-way contact with the spacecraft and successfully commanded Otter 24C, downlinking significant telemetry during early operations,” Starfish said.

However, the mission team lost contact with the oven-sized spacecraft during the commissioning process. “An investigation into the loss of communications has determined that fault protection on the satellite bus provided by Astro Digital had an error that turned off the radio and prevented re-establishing communications,” the company said.

Starfish, NASA and Astro Digital determined that the spacecraft was unrecoverable.

In today’s update, Starfish said it is working closely with Astro Digital to fix the fault protection system and update testing procedures for future Otters.

“Developing new capabilities in space requires taking on difficult challenges, learning quickly and applying those lessons to what comes next,” Starfish said. “The work completed through Otter 24C will directly contribute to the success of future Otter missions as Starfish continues developing the technologies and operations required to make satellite servicing a routine capability in orbit.”

Greg Stover, director of the Advanced Research and Technology Division at NASA Headquarters, said in a news release that “commercial capabilities like Starfish’s are critical for a competitive U.S. space economy and strong American leadership in low Earth orbit.”

“Technology demonstrations allow us to quickly test new systems, learn from their performance, and iterate so that we are pushing the boundaries of what’s possible with every mission,” Stover said. “NASA will continue taking on bold challenges in close collaboration with our industry partners.”

Several other Otter missions remain on tap — including a satellite disposal job for the Pentagon’s Space Development Agency, two commercial satellite servicing missions in geostationary Earth orbit, and two servicing missions for the U.S. Space Force.

Starfish Space is also continuing to test an Otter Pup prototype that was launched into orbit last year. Last week, it posted a video to LinkedIn showing a close approach to a target satellite known as ElaraSat. The company says it is preparing for a docking attempt “and a chance to make history.”

‘Colin From Accounts’ Maker Easy Tiger Adapting Dervla McTiernan Novel ‘Three Reasons For Revenge’ As TV Series

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Colin From Accounts producer Easy Tiger has secured TV rights for Dervla McTiernan‘s bestselling psychological thriller novel Three Reasons For Revenge.

Following what sources called a “heated auction,” the Australia-based Easy Tiger has attached Shaun Grant (The Narrow Road to the Deep North, Snowtown) to write the screenplay for a TV series. No broadcaster or streamer is attached yet, with Easy Tiger set to take it out to market.

The deal was negotiated by Dan Conaway and Tom Ishizuka from literary agency Writers House. Related Stories News 'Black Snow': Travis Fimmel Is Back On Cold Case Trail In Teaser For Season 3; Premiere Date Set In Australia Acquisitions Skydance-Owned 5 Buys UK Rights To 'Wentworth' Sequel Series

Three Reasons For Revenge hit the top of Australia’s bestseller charts after it was published earlier this year, as McTiernan’s latest novel. Watch on Deadline

The thriller tells the story of of “three seemingly unconnected people caught in a lethal web of deceit and unhealed wounds.” Per the synopsis, “Three mysterious gifts are delivered to three very different houses in Melbourne; each gift is perfectly tailored to its recipient – and each will tear apart the life of its intended victim.”

McTiernan has had several of her previous projects optioned, including debut novel The Ruin, which was set at Colin Farrell’s production house, Chapel Place Productions, back in 2019. Her thrillers What Happened to Nina? and The Murder Rule were also developed for screen, the latter at FX.

“From the moment I first read Three Reasons for Revenge I immediately envisaged it as a television series,” said writer Grant. “It is a supremely crafted thriller full of surprises, and I thank Dervla for entrusting Easy Tiger and myself with her creation.”

“There’s no doubt Dervla’s highly entertaining book will find a place not only on your shelves but on your TV screen,” added Rob Gibson, CEO of Easy Tiger. “Dervla is a master at creating a gripping thriller plot and Shaun is the perfect writer to adapt her book. We’re so delighted to be working with them both.”

Easy Tiger is one of the more prolific Australian production houses, with Patrick Brammall and Harriet Dyer’s Binge comedy Colin From Accounts breaking out in the UK on the BBC and in the U.S. on Paramount+. The romantic comedy ended this year after three seasons.

Other major Easy Tiger productions include legal drama The Twelve, Stan mystery thriller Scrublands, Netflix’s Territory, ABC’s Rake and Jack Irish and Disney movie Saving Mr Banks

Can DC’s new delegate get Congress to care about statehood?

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

When Robert White gets sworn into Congress on Jan. 3, he’ll have one thing on his mind: convincing the rest of his party that D.C. statehood belongs on the priority list.

As Democrats look forward to life in the majority, they’ve got big plans as they try to deliver on their affordability campaign promises and conduct oversight of the Trump administration. White, a 44-year-old former councilmember who was born and raised in the District, has his pitch ready.

“The agenda gets done if you have the votes — and D.C. statehood gives you more votes,” he said.

His election marks a new chapter for the District’s nonvoting delegate. As Eleanor Holmes Norton retires after more than three decades, he’ll be just the third person to hold the seat in modern times. It also comes as President Donald Trump has tried to exert more control over local affairs in the nation’s capital.

White has the delicate task of paying homage to the legacy of his former boss (he once worked as her legislative counsel) while also putting some distance between them. Norton was known as a fierce advocate for the District but lately appeared to struggle through prepared remarks as her influence diminished. Earlier in her time in Congress, she pushed through both concrete and symbolic incremental wins, such as securing delegates the right to vote in what’s known as the committee of the whole.

He said Norton “built powerfully” on the work of Walter Fauntroy, who served as delegate from 1971 to 1991. “And my job is to build on top of what Norton built.”

At a Monday event, White’s father, Robert White Sr., wore a shirt with a slogan to that effect: “FAUNTROY & NORTON & WHITE.”

A crowd was assembled outside the John A. Wilson Building that night to mark White’s departure from the D.C. Council, after a last-minute uproar over a provision in the 1970 law establishing D.C.’s delegate position. Although he had hoped to serve on the council until January, the law states a delegate must hold “no other paid public office” on the date of his election, and White ultimately decided to resign before Election Day. As he stepped up to the podium, someone shouted a rallying cry that has only grown louder after Trump deployed National Guard troops to patrol the streets last year: “Free D.C.”

White won’t be the only freshman member next Congress who can’t cast a vote on the House floor — Alicia Limtiaco is another newcomer, after beating incumbent Del. James Moylan in Guam.

One way White thinks he can further his cause is by boosting his own name recognition.

“I need to make sure I’m developing a national profile for this seat,” he said. “I’ve got to hire the right comms director with the experience to help me get it done. I’m going to use the proximity of my seat here in D.C., making sure that all the national outlets know that when other members go back to their district, I’m still here and available to do interviews. Everything that we can do, including building relationships in other media markets, including through my leadership PAC, we’re going to be doing.”

He plans to start making his case before the new Congress even begins.

“I’m already starting to put together materials for all incoming members, and talking to leadership to see if I can carve out a piece of new member orientation to talk about D.C. both being a resource to members while they’re here, but also D.C. statehood,” he said.

He knows that even in his own party, standing up for D.C. can be a tough sell. A number of moderate Democrats have voted with Republicans on bills seeking to undermine D.C.’s autonomy and roll back local criminal justice laws over the last few years.

“We have seen D.C. used as a bargaining chip by the party,” he said. “The Democratic Party has not consistently been a firewall for D.C.”

Party consensus on D.C. statehood has evolved over time, but it hasn’t always been a unifying issue. In 1993, 105 House Democrats joined 172 Republicans in voting against a bill from Norton that would have paved the way for statehood and for the addition of two senators and a representative in Congress. When the House voted on statehood bills again in 2020 and 2021, it passed with near-unanimous Democratic support but stalled in the Senate.

Asked how he would respond to Democratic peers in Congress who might say they support D.C. statehood but need to focus first on issues like affordability that more directly affect their constituents, White countered with a question of his own.

“How’s that been going without the two senators from the District of Columbia?” he said.

He sees parallels with Republicans using redistricting to gain seats in Congress.

“People need to say to their constituents, ‘Look, not only are we doing what is right under democracy, but we are helping get the votes for our agenda.’ That is what the Republicans tried to do with redistricting. So, if you think you’re going to keep playing around the margins and start winning when that hasn’t worked, we’re fooling ourselves,” he said.

The way White sees it, appeals to the principles of democracy still ring true when arguing for full representation for the District’s some 700,000 residents. But pragmatic arguments matter, too.

“When they see D.C. statehood as their ticket to securing reproductive rights, their ticket to being able to afford health health care, then they start to see D.C statehood in a different light — and that is the light that I want to make sure I’m putting on this issue now,” he said.

LIVE – Bitcoin Price Slides, Washington’s Political Balance Shifts, Trump’s Crypto Agenda at Risk

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Bitcoin price fell toward $75,700 as Democrats captured the Senate in the 2026 midterm elections, raising fresh uncertainty over Trump’s crypto agenda. But Bitcoin has now recovered to $77,700 during Asian trading hours, but remained under pressure as Nasdaq futures plunged and investors reassessed Washington’s political balance.

Democrats secured control of the Senate in a sweeping rebuke to President Donald Trump, winning key races in Texas, Ohio, North Carolina, Kansas and South Carolina. The House outcome remained closely contested, with Democrats also making significant gains.

The result threatens to complicate Trump’s push for clearer crypto regulations. Bitcoin price weakness now reflects both market risk aversion and concerns that Congress could delay legislation designed to establish a federal framework for digital assets.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

Bitcoin briefly dropped more than $2,000 after early election results pointed toward a Democratic victory, falling from nearly $78,000 to the $75,000 level. Buyers subsequently stepped in, lifting BTC toward $77,700 this morning.

The selloff extended beyond crypto markets. Nasdaq 100 futures dropped as much as 2.3%, while S&P 500 futures fell 1.3% as investors reacted to the possibility of tighter oversight of technology companies and AI infrastructure projects.

BREAKING: The S&P 500 is now on track to open below 7,500 for the first time since July 31st as markets price-in a Democrat victory in both the House and Senate.Nasdaq 100 futures are now down more than -2% tonight. pic.twitter.com/mBbq77a2mG

The dollar also weakened, while the 10-year Treasury yield slipped six basis points to around 4.90%. The moves raised concerns that a Democratic-controlled Congress could limit Trump’s tax and spending agenda, while increasing regulatory scrutiny across several industries.

Bitcoin’s decline followed an already difficult period for digital assets. The king had slipped below $77,000 earlier in the week as technology stocks weakened and investors reduced exposure to riskier assets.

The election adds another source of uncertainty, although Bitcoin’s recovery from its overnight low shows people have not fully abandoned the market.

The biggest crypto-specific concern involves the Digital Asset Market CLARITY Act, which aims to establish clearer rules governing digital assets and divide oversight responsibilities between U.S. regulators.

The legislation failed to advance through a Senate procedural vote in September, falling short of the 60 votes required. Democrats opposed the measure over concerns about ethics provisions and Trump’s crypto-related business interests.

A Democratic-controlled Senate could make the bill’s path more difficult, particularly if lawmakers prioritize stronger safeguards and additional oversight of the crypto industry.

The election also gives Democrats greater power to investigate the administration and examine potential conflicts of interest involving Trump’s digital asset ventures. Congressional scrutiny could intensify even without new legislation.

However, the result does not automatically mean crypto regulation will become more restrictive. Democrats have supported regulatory frameworks for digital assets, but disagree with Republicans over enforcement, consumer protections and safeguards against political conflicts.

The immediate risk is that negotiations become slower and more complicated, leaving crypto firms without the regulatory clarity they have sought.

Bitcoin price direction will likely depend on whether the election-driven selloff spreads into a deeper market correction. A sustained recovery in Nasdaq could help Bitcoin regain momentum, particularly if investors begin treating the election result as a temporary source of uncertainty rather than a lasting threat to risk assets.

On the other hand, continued weakness in technology stocks could pressure BTC and altcoins further. Investors will also watch whether Democrats pursue aggressive investigations or push for substantial changes to crypto legislation.

The political shift could create a more difficult environment for Trump’s crypto agenda during the final two years of his presidency. Yet a divided government may also limit sweeping policy changes from either party.

For now, Bitcoin price remains caught between broader market weakness and uncertainty over U.S. crypto regulation. The election has changed the political landscape, but the next moves in Congress will determine whether the impact becomes a lasting obstacle for the industry.

US midterm elections 2026 live: Democrats win House, securing control of Congress

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Follow live coverage of the 2026 US midterm elections, including key congressional races, crypto-backed candidates and what the results could mean for digital asset regulation.

The crypto industry is beginning to learn whether President Donald Trump’s Republican Party has managed to retain control of the US Congress, determining his ability to advance his legislative agenda during the final two years of his term.

Follow the latest developments in congressional races attracting crypto industry spending, election results and reactions from prediction markets. Cointelegraph is using live election results data and race calls from the Associated Press. Updates appear in reverse chronological order.

Democrats have won control of the US House of Representatives, according to the Associated Press, flipping a chamber Republicans have held since 2023. Rep. Hakeem Jeffries is in line to become Speaker.

Control of the House gives Democrats the committee chairs and subpoena power. NBC says that includes oversight of Trump’s family and business ventures.

Democrat Troy Jackson defeated Republican Sen. Susan Collins in Maine, according to the Associated Press.

Collins was one of four Republicans to oppose advancing the CLARITY Act in September, expressing concerns over its impact on bank deposits.

Unlike Collins, Jackson has expressed support for clear cryptocurrency legislation in the run-up to the election. According to Stand with Crypto, which rates Jackson as “very pro crypto,” he called to end the debanking of “lawful crypto businesses, and ensure Americans’ right to self-custody digital assets.”

“My message is congratulations to the Democrats, and I look forward to working with you for the good of our country,” President Donald Trump told CBS News’ senior White House correspondent Weijia Jiang.

Democrats have won control of the US Senate, according to the Associated Press.

The majority was secured after Democrat Josh Turek won the Senate race in Iowa, bringing Democrats and independents who caucus with them to 51 seats.

The result reshapes the outlook for the stalled CLARITY Act and determines which lawmakers lead committees overseeing the industry.

Democratic control could also bring more congressional scrutiny of Trump-linked crypto businesses and regulators’ decisions. Control of the Senate could also affect confirmation of future presidential nominees to regulatory posts.

“It’s too early to say exactly what this means for crypto, but one thing is clear: nothing will pass without cross-partisan support,” Jerome de Tychey, president of Ethereum France and co-founder of EthCC, told Cointelegraph.

Democrats are now in control of 50 Senate seats — one short of a majority — after flipping five seats in GOP states, some that hadn’t seen a Democratic senator in power for decades.

Earlier, Decision Desk HQ projected the Democratic Party to win a majority in the US House of Representatives.

In Ohio, Democrat Sherrod Brown, a longtime crypto critic, beat incumbent Republican Senator Jon Husted in a race that has attracted approximately $63 million in crypto industry spending.

That includes about $56 million from Fairshake and $7 million from the Winklevoss-backed Digital Freedom Fund, with both groups spending to support Husted or oppose Brown.

Crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto characterizes Brown as strongly opposed to cryptocurrency, citing 17 statements and his 2024 vote against overturning the SEC’s SAB 121 crypto custody accounting guidance.

Brown was also targeted by crypto industry spending in 2024, when Fairshake affiliate Defend American Jobs spent more than $40 million supporting Republican Bernie Moreno, who defeated Brown in that year’s Senate election.

Democrat James Talarico has won Texas’ US Senate race against Republican Ken Paxton, according to the Associated Press, flipping another Republican-held seat.

Talarico becomes the first Democrat to win a statewide election in Texas since 1994. As of 11 pm ET, he led Paxton by approximately 1.09 million votes, with 56.1% to Paxton’s 42.2%, according to Decision Desk HQ. Counting was continuing.

Paxton received support from the Winklevoss-backed Digital Freedom Fund, although both candidates earned A ratings from crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto.

Paxton has called for the passage of the CLARITY Act, arguing that clear regulations are necessary to maintain US competitiveness in digital assets. In a June statement, he said the country must become the “crypto capital of the world” and support innovation and growth.

Talarico has similarly called for clear regulations that protect consumers while allowing innovation. As a Texas state representative, he also voted in 2025 to establish a state strategic Bitcoin reserve, according to legislative records cited by Stand With Crypto.

Democrat Annie Andrews has defeated Republican Sen. Darline Graham in South Carolina, according to the Associated Press, giving Democrats another pickup.

Andrews’ victory ends more than two decades of Republican control of the seat. The last time South Carolina elected a Democrat to the Senate was in 1998.

Following their victory in North Carolina, Democrats would need two further Republican-held seats to reach a majority, provided they retain all their Democratic-held seats.

Decision Desk HQ has projected the Democratic Party to win a majority in the US House of Representatives.

Democrats and allied independents stood at 45 Senate seats as of 10:02 pm ET, six short of the 51 needed for a majority.

Their path includes retaining Democratic-held seats in Oregon, Michigan and Minnesota and flipping three additional Republican-held seats. Democrats have already picked up North Carolina.

NBC News has projected that Democrat Annie Andrews will defeat incumbent Republican Senator Darline Graham in South Carolina in a major upset. Associated Press has not yet called this race.

Republican Representative Andy Barr has won Kentucky’s US Senate race, sending a supporter of the CLARITY and GENIUS Acts to the upper chamber.

Fairshake-affiliated political action committees spent more than $7 million supporting Barr in the Republican primary. Barr has also introduced legislation aimed at protecting lawful businesses, including crypto firms, from debanking.

Democrat Juliana Stratton has won Illinois’ US Senate race, succeeding retiring Democratic Sen. Dick Durbin.

Stratton advanced through the Democratic primary despite substantial opposition from crypto-backed PAC Fairshake, which had spent more than $5.5 million against her by early March.

Republican Representative Harriet Hageman has won Wyoming’s US Senate race against Democrat James Byrd, succeeding retiring crypto advocate Senator Cynthia Lummis.

Lummis, who chairs the Senate Banking Subcommittee on Digital Assets, sponsored the BITCOIN Act of 2025 to establish a US strategic Bitcoin reserve and has supported the CLARITY and GENIUS Acts.

Hageman has also backed cryptocurrency legislation, voting to pass the CLARITY and GENIUS Acts in 2025 and the FIT21 market structure bill in 2024. She also cosponsored legislation seeking to prohibit the Federal Reserve from issuing a central bank digital currency, according to Stand With Crypto. The advocacy group lists no documented position on cryptocurrency for Byrd.

Kalshi and Polymarket traders now give Democrats a 99% chance of winning Senate control and price House control at nearly 100%. It came as Democrats flipped North Carolina’s Senate seat, while holding Georgia and New Hampshire.

Prediction markets increasingly favored Democrats taking control of both chambers of Congress on Tuesday evening.

As of 8:05 pm ET, the Blockchain Association’s election dashboard showed Democratic Senate-control odds at 94% on Kalshi and 93% on Polymarket, up from 75% and 75.5%, respectively, earlier in the day. Democratic House-control odds stood at 98% and 99%.

At the time, Democrats and allied independents have secured 42 Senate seats to Republicans’ 31.

Massachusetts Senator Ed Markey and Delaware Senator Chris Coons, both Democrats, win reelection, according to the Associated Press.

Markey voted against the GENIUS stablecoin bill in 2025, while Coons has criticized President Donald Trump’s crypto ventures, calling his latest memecoin-holder event “corruption.”

Democratic Sen. Jon Ossoff has won reelection in Georgia, beating Republican Representative Mike Collins.

Ossoff expressed support for impeaching Trump in April 2025 over plans to host a private dinner for top holders of the president’s TRUMP memecoin, arguing that the arrangement amounted to selling access to the presidency.

Maylea Ma, deputy general counsel at 1inch, said she expects federal agencies to drive the next phase of crypto regulation.

The election could reshape the path forward for the stalled CLARITY Act, but Ma said “real movement” in US digital-asset regulation over the next few years will come from the US Securities and Exchange Commission, Commodity Futures Trading Commission and Treasury, rather than Congress.

Ma said those agencies were increasingly engaging with crypto projects. However, agency engagement does not provide the stability the industry had hoped to secure through legislation.

“The long-term goal, of course, remains a statute that cannot be reversed every time a government changes,” said Ma.

Democratic Rep. Debbie Wasserman Schultz wins Florida’s 20th Congressional District. Wasserman Schultz voted against the CLARITY crypto market structure bill when it passed the House in July 2025.

Democratic Senator Mark Warner has won reelection in Virginia, defeating Republican challenger Bert Mizusawa. Warner was widely favored to win.

Warner voted against advancing the CLARITY Act in September, arguing that crypto legislation needs safeguards against President Donald Trump personally benefiting from the industry. Warner supports establishing federal rules for digital assets, with his opposition to the bill focused on its ethics protections.

The first polls in the US midterm elections closed at 6 pm ET in parts of Indiana and Kentucky. The remaining polls in both states close at 7 pm ET.

Polls also close at 7 pm ET in Georgia and Virginia, followed by Ohio at 7:30 pm ET, where Republican Sen. Jon Husted faces Democrat Sherrod Brown in a race drawing major crypto industry spending.

More than 57 million Americans had cast ballots as of Monday, with early voting surpassing 2022 levels in several closely watched states, according to the Associated Press.

Current balance of power in the US House and Senate. Source: Blockchain Association

Republican Sen. Pete Ricketts faces independent challenger Dan Osborn in Nebraska, where crypto political action committee Fairshake had spent nearly $11 million supporting Ricketts, according to an Oct. 27 report by the Washington Examiner.

More recent spending data shows Fairshake-affiliated crypto super PAC Defend American Jobs spent approximately $13.5 million opposing Osborn, according to Federal Election Commission data compiled by Bankrolled as of Nov. 3.

Ricketts, a member of the Senate Banking Committee, has supported cryptocurrency legislation including the CLARITY Act and GENIUS Act. He voted to advance the CLARITY Act out of committee in May and supported an unsuccessful Senate procedural vote on the legislation in September, according to crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto.

Arsenal face Slavia Prague in the Champions League tonight, exactly a year on from their last visit to the Czech Republic.

When these two sides met on November 4, 2025, it finished 3-0 to Arsenal as Mikel Merino added to Bukayo Saka’s penalty with a second half brace.

While, the Gunners go into this meeting on the back of a huge win over Liverpool to end their long Anfield hoodoo, last week’s shock Carabao Cup exit at the hands of Fleetwood Town serves as a reminder against complacency.

That said, Mikel Arteta’s side are big favourites to maintain their unbeaten start to this season’s league phase, having beaten Napoli and Lille, and earning a 2-2 draw with Bayern Munich a fortnight ago.

Prague, meanwhile, have picked up just one point from their opening three Champions League fixtures, losing to Lens and competition debutants Sabah, but did claw back a two-goal deficit against Fenerbahce to earn a point.

The Czech side have never beaten Gunners in five previous attempts, failing to score in any of the home meetings which also includes losing 4-0 in an Europa League quarter-final second leg back in April 2021.

The Rise and Rise of Adéla

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

It’s 9 p.m. on a Tuesday and I’m four floors underground, at a sweaty central London club. Surrounding me are roughly 2,000 Gen-Zers in pink wigs and fishnets chanting—between surreptitious vape puffs—the lyrics to a then unreleased song. “I got boys, like a vending machine,” voices boom around me as Adéla, her lithe ballet dancer’s figure clad in a denim-and-rhinestone bralette and hot pants, flips her hair Baywatch-style and kicks her leg so far it flies all the way back over her head. (They’re the kind of moves that, if I attempted them, paramedics would have to be called.) There’s peak-Britney choreo for the bangers, peak-Xtina vocals on the ballads, cheeky foul-mouthed banter in between—she commands the stage like a veteran.

Dilara Findikoglu silk corset jacket and skirt. Heavenly London zirconia and velvet choker.

Twelve hours later, the Slovakian pop renegade bounces around a light-filled rehearsal room at the English National Ballet School, spinning like a whirling dervish in a black Chanel gown for her British Vogue shoot, still in the thick of an August heatwave. If Adéla is tired, she doesn’t let on. “I think because I’m 22… that helps,” she says with a mischievous grin as we sit down afterwards, running her talon-like acrylic nails through her signature pink locks. (She’s recently dyed her hair a deeper, dirtier magenta in place of her previous pastel hue: “My fans say I gain more power the pinker my hair gets.”) Her gaze falls on the ballet barres and mirrored walls, and she pauses: Adéla has history here, having moved from Bratislava to study dance in these exact rooms as a 15-year-old. “I met one of my best friends, Luca, here—he was my first fan,” she recalls. “He was like, ‘I feel like you could be a pop star.’ So I have fond memories of this place.”

Adéla’s journey to becoming this year’s most-buzzed-about music breakout has been uncommonly well-documented. Originally cast in 2023 as a contestant for the K-pop reality show that formed Katseye, she was booted out early partly because—despite becoming a fan favorite in Netflix’s subsequent docuseries—the judges sensed she was destined for solo stardom. She seized upon the exposure, independently releasing her early songs and music videos and building a cult following among internet pop savants, then signing with Capitol Records in 2025 and releasing her debut EP, The Provocateur, last summer to rave reviews. (The eye-popping album artwork features Adéla pulling up her dress and urinating in the street; you can understand why she was never built for the clean-cut confines of a manufactured pop group.)

Marc Jacobs slip dress, T-shirt, and tights. Ebay pre-loved boots. Completedworks necklace.

And when I meet her, Adéla is on the precipice of her biggest leap yet: just over a week later, she’s releasing her debut album, Prima, and its third single, “Ain’t in LA,” is surging up the charts globally. The song, which recalls the world-dominating hits of Miley Cyrus’s Bangerz era, is a celebration of the cool girls in “unknown cities” around the world—“It’s all a lie, the baddest bitches ain’t in LA,” she sings on the euphoric chorus—and the video features Adéla running through the streets of Bratislava, posing with breakdancing kids in Slovak folk costumes, and rolling around with her mom and younger sister at home. (Both are in town for the concert and sit to watch her pose for the shoot, visibly beaming with pride.) The most recent single, “Nicole Kidman,” is also a viral sensation, with a video referencing some of the actor’s most iconic roles (“Moulin Rouge, my corset is so tight”) and her internet-favorite AMC adt; Kidman herself is a fan, calling Adéla “so talented.”

Her label didn’t think “Ain’t in LA” should be a single and she had to push for it, she says. “There are so many forces at play that pull you a thousand different directions when you’re an artist—and they want what’s best for you. It’s not like they’re evil, but they might pull you away from something really great.” Her assessment of why they might not have understood it initially is equally frank: “I think the people in my label didn’t necessarily resonate with it because they’re LA rich kids. No shade, but they are. And that’s what a lot of the music industry is.”

Chanel leather dress, fringed viscose skirt, and laminated leather shoes.

It’s that candor—and wry sense of humor—that has endeared her to her rapidly growing fanbase: nearly six million across Instagram and TikTok (and counting). She’s still learning to navigate the intensity of that fame, especially as some fans have pushed back about her switching up her sound from the gnarly electroclash of her EP to the more polished pop of Prima: “Going on socials and seeing what everybody’s saying… I guess I’m going to learn how to deal with that.” She visibly steels herself. “It’s definitely tough.”

That meteoric rise has already turned her into a fashion-world darling: working with creative director Chris Horan—Charli xcx’s longtime stylist and a key architect of the Brat-osphere—she’s already scored a Mac Cosmetics campaign, a front-row seat at Saint Laurent’s Paris Fashion Week show, and a killer Marc Jacobs look at the CFDA Awards, complete with skyscraper platforms. “I had to hold onto somebody [to walk in them],” she says, with a deep laugh. “They kept passing me to each other like I was a baby the entire event. But I’ll do anything for a fashion moment.”

Adéla is a devoted scholar of pop stardom and lights up when I mention the crowd the previous night cosplaying as her in their wigs and leg warmers. (The most popular look? Hot-pink T-shirts reading: “The baddest bitches are in London.”) “That’s definitely a thing that I think every good pop star has,” she says. “When I was nine, I used to wear a half-down ponytail every day of my life because of Ariana Grande.” It feels like that’s who Adéla is doing it all for: not the fans online, not entirely for herself today, but for that young girl growing up in Bratislava with posters of Ariana and Miley and Beyoncé on her wall. “I wanted to make an album that I knew if I listened to it when I was nine, 10, 11, I would’ve been like, ‘This is sick.’”

Hair, Masaki Kameda; makeup, Bea Sweet. Nails: Ella Vivii. With thanks to the English National Ballet School.

Winter Hiking Boot FAQ

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

If you plan to hike this winter, buy insulated, waterproof winter hiking boots to protect your feet and keep them warm. Regular hiking boots intended for three-season conditions, mids, or trail runners lack enough insulation and may not stay waterproof in wet snow, puddles, or slush. Even regular leather hiking boots treated with waterproofing products can absorb moisture, becoming cold and potentially freezing during a long outing. This can lead to discomfort, frostnip, or frostbite in severe conditions.

Insulated winter hiking boots are a much better option because they have waterproof construction, rubber or waterproof synthetic lowers, and insulation that helps retain warmth even if moisture enters from above or accumulates from perspiration. While waterproof membranes such as Gore-Tex can reduce water entry, they cannot prevent dampness from sweating or the chilling effect foot perspiration can cause in cold winter weather. When that happens, insulation remains the most important factor. A damp insulated boot can still retain warmth better than an uninsulated boot that has absorbed water and begun to freeze.

When choosing insulated winter hiking boots, you’ll want boots for the temperatures you expect to encounter, usually between -10°F and 35°F, although you may encounter even colder temperatures. People with cold feet may want to get more heavily insulated boots to stay warm. The temperature ratings that winter hiking boots come with assume active use, so it pays to get a boot that is even warmer if you have to stop and stand around for a while. Say, for instance, a companion is injured. You’ll want boots with enough foot insulation to stay with them until help arrives.

Winter boots generally fall into three categories:

Each type is designed for different conditions and activities, from winter hiking and snowshoeing to ice climbing, snowmobiling, and ice fishing. The best choice depends on how much you walk, how cold and wet the conditions are, and whether you need to use traction devices such as microspikes, snowshoes, or crampons.

The waterproof/breathable liners, like Gore-Tex, built into cold-weather boots usually do a good job of keeping moisture out, especially when the boots are new. But it doesn’t hurt to add extra waterproofing, like Sno-Seal, to the outside of the boots, especially along the seams, for added protection. Waterproof/breathable liners do break down with use, and it’s good to have a backup strategy. These liners also provide poor breathability in winter, and your socks will likely get damp from perspiration if you’re active. While not ideal, this is normal, and the added insulation will keep your feet warm.

If you plan to hike or snowshoe in winter, you’ll want insulated, waterproof boots designed primarily for walking, not more technical activities like mountain climbing or ice-climbing, or sedentary activities like snowmobiling or ice fishing.

Winter hiking boots look like regular hiking boots, mid-height boots, or even boots with coverage above the ankle, but they’re all waterproof and insulated. For most winter hiking and snowshoeing, boots that extend just above the ankle are the most comfortable and practical. The more calf coverage you have, the warmer your feet will remain.

Winter hiking boots should have laces instead of slip-on designs or zippers so you can tighten the fit. When walking on snow or ice, lug depth and rubber type matter less than your ability to use traction aids like microspikes or snowshoes, which bite into the surface and keep you from slipping. For instance, anti-slip rubber soles designed to prevent slipping on urban sidewalks are virtually worthless if you need to wear microspikes for traction most of the time.

Synthetic insulated boots are usually lighter and more water-resistant than leather boots. The insulation will be built into the boot and not removable, which keeps the weight down and makes the boots easier to walk in. The amount of insulation you need depends on the temperature, how active you will be, and whether you will walk continuously or stand still for long periods.

Winter boots are commonly offered with 200-gram or 400-gram Thinsulate insulation, although PrimaLoft or Aerogel insulation is also used and provides the same level of warmth retention. The 200g and 400g quantities refer to the insulation’s thickness or density, not its weight inside your boots.

As a general guide, 200-gram insulation suits active use in temperatures around 0 to 10 degrees Fahrenheit, while 400-gram insulation is better suited to temperatures between approximately -20 and -40 degrees Fahrenheit. These estimates assume that you are walking rather than standing around. Individual experiences vary considerably.

We recommend the following insulated 200-gram and 400-gram winter hiking boots:

Regardless of insulation level, check that your boots work properly with your microspikes, snowshoes, or crampons. Traction devices should not create excessive pressure on your toes or the front of your boots, since this can cause discomfort and blisters. This is often the case with lighter-weight winter hiking boots or mids that have soft uppers.

Mountaineering boots are designed for steep, technical terrain and ice climbing. They have rigid soles that support crampons and allow climbers to stand on small edges of ice or rock. They are also generally heavier, less flexible, and less comfortable for ordinary hiking than insulated winter boots. They can also be quite bulky and may not be compatible with microspikes or even snowshoe bindings. Mountaineering boots I recommend.

Mountaineering boots are available in single-layer and double-layer designs. Single-layer boots, like those listed above, are intended primarily for day trips in exposed alpine terrain. Double-layer boots have a removable liner and are best for overnight trips because you can remove a damp liner and sleep with it, preventing it from freezing overnight.  They’ve largely fallen out of use, but if you’re going someplace where you need them, you can often rent them from mountain guides, especially if you’ve hired a guide service.

If you need a full crampon compatible with the flexible soles of insulated winter hiking boots, look for one with a “flexible leaf spring or center bar,” like the Camp Stalker Universal Crampon. This flexible center bar connects the front spikes to the rear spike assembly. The flexible bar prevents the crampon from snapping in half when used with boots that have more flexible soles.

Pac boots usually have a rubber lower section, a leather or synthetic upper, and a removable felt liner. They are designed for very cold, wet conditions, but their warmth comes with several hiking disadvantages. Pac boots are heavy, bulky, extend well above the ankle, and provide limited ankle support.

They suit activities that require little walking, such as ice fishing and snowmobiling.

Their large size can also make it difficult to fit with microspikes, crampons, and most snowshoes. For active winter hiking, insulated hiking boots are usually a better choice.

Boot warmth ratings are useful for comparing products, but they are not standardized. Manufacturers may test their boots differently, and a rating cannot account for every person or condition.

Your experience will depend on factors such as:

Read customer reviews and look for boots that have been sold for several seasons. Recommendations from experienced hikers and people you trust can also be useful.

Beyond insulation and waterproofing, several design details can improve comfort and performance.

A manufacturer’s temperature rating provides a rough indication of warmth, but it should not be treated as a guarantee.

A sturdy toe cap protects your feet and helps prevent the front of the boot from collapsing under tight-fitting microspikes or crampons. A collapsed toe box can cause pressure and blisters.

A gusseted tongue is attached along its sides to the inside of the boot. It helps keep water from entering through the laces when you step into deep snow or puddles.

The cuff is the upper edge of the boot around your lower leg. A soft, comfortable cuff helps retain heat and prevents snow from entering when you walk through deep snow.

Deep, well-spaced lugs provide traction on packed and loose snow. Rubber compounds and tread patterns vary, but a durable sole such as Vibram can provide reliable grip. On ice, you will generally need microspikes or crampons in addition to the boot tread.

A gaiter ring near the toe allows you to attach the front hook of your gaiters. If your boots do not have one, you can often attach gaiters to the laces instead.

Winter boots should not be tight. A cramped boot restricts circulation and can make your feet colder. Your toes should have enough room to move, but your foot should not slide excessively from side to side. Your heel should remain secure when the boot is fully laced.

Try on winter boots with the socks you plan to wear. If you wear thick winter socks, try them on during the fitting process. When between sizes, it is usually safer to choose the larger size. You can fill a small amount of extra space with thicker socks or higher-volume insoles, such as Superfeet. You can’t make a boot that’s too small larger.

Report: Liverpool's next superstar on brink of signing, they've been told 'yes'

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Liverpool have been given the green light. Their perfect midfield signing is right around the corner.

Liverpool unquestionably need to make some sort of midfield move. It feels like they're short on numbers, honestly, especially after Curtis Jones left in the summer without any sort of replacement.

But that's only half of it. The real reason they feel short on numbers is that everyone wants to play the same role - an attacking no.8. Andoni Iraola has taken advantage of the fact Trey Nyoni is still learning the game to convert him into a holding player but make no mistake, that was seen as his role as little as six months ago.

There's very little variety in there, then, and importantly, there's no real controlling player. Liverpool have a group of midfielders who aren't good at winning the ball back, nor can they plug gaps through the middle particularly well.

So isn't it fortunate that the ideal midfielder is ready to join?

Lamine Camara green lightWe’re enormous fans of Lamine Camara and his data last season suggested he would soon explode into elite-level form. We think it’s safe to say that’s happened as he’s been a true star of Ligue 1 thus far this campaign. He’s also a player that Liverpool have wanted to sign. We thought it might happen right at the end of the transfer window but the Reds ultimately couldn’t justify a late move. What they did do, however, is talk to AS Monaco and Camara’s representatives to open the door to a move in 2027. That’s grown to a good position, it seems, as Football Insider claim that the green light has been given.

We’re enormous fans of Lamine Camara and his data last season suggested he would soon explode into elite-level form. We think it’s safe to say that’s happened as he’s been a true star of Ligue 1 thus far this campaign.

He’s also a player that Liverpool have wanted to sign. We thought it might happen right at the end of the transfer window but the Reds ultimately couldn’t justify a late move.

What they did do, however, is talk to AS Monaco and Camara’s representatives to open the door to a move in 2027. That’s grown to a good position, it seems, as Football Insider claim that the green light has been given.

They suggest that both Monaco and Camara welcome the offer from Liverpool. It’s as good as a ‘yes’ at this stage - a sign that the Reds have the deal ready to be sealed as long as they put up the kind of money they’ve indicated they will.

It’s important to note that nothing has been agreed, nor has a formal offer been made. Liverpool could ultimately move on from Camara and decide to try someone else as their next midfielder.

But the Senegal star is an option. A strong option. He’s effectively there to be signed should the Reds want him and out suspicion is that they ultimately will do.

Former Manchester United striker Wayne Rooney was full of praise for one player against Roma while criticising another.

Although United managed to pull off a 3-2 victory over the Italian side, it was a disappointing performance for long periods.

United were 2-0 down after just 18 minutes, heading into the break with a mountain to climb.

Bruno Fernandes struck from the penalty spot after 58 minutes, giving the Red Devils hope heading into the end of the game.

A crazy few minutes from Matheus Cunha allowed United to turn the game on its head, with the Brazilian making it 3-2 with just 5 minutes remaining.

In added time, Cunha was then sent off, but thankfully for United and Michael Carrick, it didn't matter, as they held on to secure a 3-2 win.

Speaking on both Amad Diallo and young winger Shea Lacey, Rooney compared the two, with the former needing to add more end product to his game.

Rooney, speaking live on Amazon Prime after the game, praised youngster Lacey after he came off the bench to make a serious impact, setting up Cunha for the winner.

"That's a great touch inside and a wonderful pass, and the finish is incredible. If you look at it again, you probably think, can he square it and play your teammate in? But for me, it's all about that first touch [from Lacey], takes the defender out and that pass is incredible. We've said about Amad, Amad does some really good things, but it's that end product, that is what takes you to the next level. And the glimpses of what I've seen from Lacey when he has come on, especially this season, he has got something in there, that little touch and little pass which United needed to get them over the line in this game."

Manchester United manager Carrick is in a situation where he needs some inspiration from somewhere.

The win over Roma isn't going to cover all the cracks. The Red Devils are still on a horrendous run, and they were still close to losing that game.

Their form in the Premier League simply hasn't been good enough, and it could be time for Carrick to unleash one of United's best academy players.

Lacey has looked incredibly impressive when given an opportunity, and there's little reason not to give him a chance now.

Manchester United recorded their first win in 10 matches with a much-needed 3-2 comeback success against Roma in the Champions League

If Lacey had players ahead of him who were performing at a strong level, then you could understand him not being given much game time at Old Trafford. The reality is that a number of players ahead of Lacey are currently underperforming, and the Manchester club are struggling immensely.

As mentioned, Carrick needs some inspiration from somewhere, and the answer could be in Lacey after his assist against Roma.

The young winger is an incredibly exciting product who doesn't look out of place at senior level.

Amad simply isn't performing to the level required for United to turn things around, so there's little reason for Carrick not to give Lacey a start at Premier League level.

Forget About Lankshear: Tottenham Already Have Next Harry Kane In Academy

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Roberto De Zerbi has got his Tottenham squad firing.

Three wins on the spin have seen Spurs move from rock bottom and up to ninth in the Premier League table, with supporters now looking up rather than down. They recorded a much-needed first win of the season against Coventry City before following that up by beating Chelsea 2-0 at Stamford Bridge. De Zerbi's men then put Crystal Palace to the sword with a comfortable 2-0 success on Saturday.

Although Tottenham are in-form and starting to find their groove, it does feel as though De Zerbi still has an issue up front.

Dominic Solanke has been disappointing this season, having scored just ONCE in nine Premier League appearances. That came in the 1-1 draw against Manchester United last month, as the forward headed home late to rescue a point for Spurs.

Apart from that, Solanke has flattered to deceive. The ex-Bournemouth man has underperformed his expected goals (xG) by -1.3 and has missed three big chances this season, which is among the most in the top flight.

Solanke's form has seen some Tottenham fans admit selling Will Lankshear was a mistake and have since called for the club to bring him back in January. Lankshear left Spurs to sign for Middlesborough in a £10million plus add-ons deal in the summer.

Since his move to Boro, Lankshear has been in inspired form, netting an impressive 13 goals in 13 Championship appearances this season, and he is the division's top scorer by quite a distance. His impressive goalscoring run has seen Lankshear compared to ex-Tottenham forward and now-Ballon d'Or winner Harry Kane, and it's no surprise given his start to the season.

But Spurs might be justified in selling Lankshear now there is another gem coming through the club's academy. And that is a certain 17-year-old sensation called Oliver Boast, who has been in electric form for the Under 18s this season.

A powerful 6ft 1in centre forward, Boast is catching the eye at Tottenham for his performances in the U18s, having scored a staggering 16 goals and provided four assists in just 12 appearances across all competitions this season. On Saturday, Boast scored another brace - his fifth of the season - during Tottenham U18s' 3-1 win at Bournemouth.

Boast, who signed his first professional contract at Tottenham in the summer, is the top scorer in the U18 Premier League by quite a distance and could become Spurs' next Kane.

There is no doubt Boast has the potential to be that, having scored all types of goals for the U18s. Like Kane, he can drop in and create for his teammates, as well as driving into the box and finding the net from close range, like a proper goalscorer often does.

When he scored FOUR against West Brom back in September, all of Boast's goals were first-time finishes in the box. He had no time to think about any of them but showed great striker instincts and was clinical in front of goal.

Although there is no doubt that Boast is not ready to be chucked in to first-team action just yet, he is definitely one to watch for the future and could prove Tottenham right by selling Lankshear with him coming through the academy.

Lights Out, Zoomies On: Why Dogs Go Wild at Bedtime

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

We just sent a welcome email your way with a quick overview of what to expect (including those vital recall alerts).

Dogster is reader-supported. When you buy via links on our site, we may earn a commission at no cost to you. Read about privacy.

While nighttime zoomies are a well-recognized phenomenon in the feline world, it turns out dogs can also get a little over-excited at the prospect of bedtime. Just as the house is settling down for the night, a dog may start bouncing around, digging into the blankets, or demanding attention.

It also turns out that there isn't a single explanation for this evening burst of energy; there are several. The bed itself can be exciting, particularly because it smells like a favorite person and offers a chance for interaction. In other cases, a dog may simply have energy left to burn. Understanding the possible reasons can help explain why bedtime sometimes turns into playtime.

Dogs spend much of their time looking up at people from the floor. Climbing onto a bed changes that perspective, bringing a dog much closer to its owner's face and making interaction easier.

That alone can be enough to spark a playful mood. A dog may drop into a play bow, bark for attention, or bounce around in an attempt to encourage its person to join in.

Being face to face can make play feel more inviting, particularly for dogs that enjoy close interaction with their owners. What looks like a sudden burst of energy may simply be a dog making the most of the opportunity to engage on a different level.

A bed carries the scent of the person who sleeps in it, making it an especially interesting place for a dog to explore. Dogs rely heavily on their noses to understand their surroundings, and familiar smells can be particularly appealing.

For a dog that enjoys spending time with its owner, the bed may be one of the most comforting and interesting places in the home. Rolling around on the sheets or sniffing the blankets lets a dog investigate those familiar scents while enjoying a spot associated with its favorite person.

That combination of comfort, familiarity, and excitement can help explain why some dogs become especially playful once they climb onto the mattress.

For dogs that love burrowing, a bed covered in sheets and blankets can feel like an invitation to play. Digging, wriggling underneath the covers, and pushing through folds of fabric can all become part of the fun.

Some breeds are particularly inclined toward this kind of behavior. Dachshunds, for example, were bred to pursue animals such as badgers and weasels into their burrows. Their instinct to dig and tunnel can make a pile of blankets especially appealing. Meanwhile, Chihuahuas seem to be particularly fond of burrowing into the warmth and security of their humanâs bed.

A dog that discovers the pleasure of diving between the sheets may quickly turn it into a regular bedtime activity.

Dogs are often quick to learn which behaviors get a reaction from their owners. Climbing onto the bed puts them right where they're difficult to ignore, particularly if they start bouncing around or nudging someone for attention.

Bedtime can also signal that the day is winding down and an opportunity for cuddling is approaching. A dog that enjoys being close to its owner may become excited at the prospect of spending that time together.

If playful behavior consistently earns attention, a dog may be encouraged to repeat it. What begins as a burst of excitement can become a familiar part of the nightly routine. Even if this nightly ritual isnât one you welcome, waving arms and raised voices often count as a positive interaction for a dog, so if youâre trying to get your dog off the bed, be careful your methods align with your message.

Sometimes, a dog becomes especially energetic at bedtime simply because theyâre not ready to settle down. If it hasn't had enough exercise or stimulation during the day, the bed may become an outlet for that leftover energy.

A dog that is still eager to run, jump, and play may need more activity before the household turns in for the night. An evening walk or a game of tug-of-war can help provide an opportunity to burn off some energy before bedtime.

A dog that's had enough exercise and stimulation is more likely to be ready to relax, rather than treating the mattress as a playground.

Many owners enjoy sleeping alongside their dogs, while others prefer to keep their beds pet-free. Sharing a bed can feel cozy and comforting, and having a dog nearby may also be soothing for people who experience anxiety or other mental health challenges.

There are practical considerations, too. Dogs can carry dirt, bacteria, and parasites into the bed, and some owners would rather not have fur on their sheets or pillows.

Ultimately, whether a dog is allowed to sleep on the bed comes down to what works for the household. If itâs not a problem, then itâs not a problem. There's no single right choice, and a dog can still enjoy plenty of affection and companionship without sharing the mattress.

If you do want your bed to be a dog-free zone, the simplest solution is to keep the bedroom door closed. But if youâre simply looking for a way to get them off the bed without it turning into a party, a calm and quiet approach is best. A single command or using a slip-lead to silently guide them off the bed will be far more effective than shouting and pointing.

A dog getting frisky on the bed is often responding to a combination of familiar scents, opportunities for attention, and the chance to play somewhere comfortable. Blankets can add to the appeal, especially for dogs that enjoy burrowing.

If the behavior seems excessive, however, it may be worth looking at how much exercise and stimulation the dog gets during the day. Adding a walk or play session before bed can help make the transition to sleep easier.

For many dogs, the bed is simply an exciting place to be close to their favorite person. With a little preparation, bedtime can still end in a peaceful night's sleep rather than an impromptu wrestling match.

Diagnosing a Ghost – The Mysterious Feline Disease Whose Signs Appear & Disappear

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Catster is reader-supported. When you buy via links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission at no cost to you. Learn more.

Waxing and waning signs, vague symptoms that come and go. A cat that is on deathâs door one moment, running around the next. Detecting illness in cats can be difficult enough, but when the body starts attacking itself, diagnosis becomes even more of a challenge.

In an autoimmune disease, a catâs immune system malfunctions and begins attacking the body it is meant to protect. Fortunately, autoimmune disease is relatively uncommon in cats, but it can be serious and difficult to diagnose and treat. Because the signs tend to be vague and can come and go, they are easy to overlook, which makes it worth knowing what to watch for and when to involve a vet.

A healthy immune system protects the body from germs such as viruses and bacteria by sending out specialized cells to locate and destroy them. It also helps clear away damaged or diseased cells. These immune cells use protein signals on the surface of the bodyâs own cells to recognize them as safe. In autoimmune disease, a signaling error causes the immune system to treat the catâs own cells as a threat, and healthy, working tissue gets attacked and destroyed.

Cats can develop these problems much as people do. They can affect any part of the body, including the internal organs, blood, and skin, and there are many different types. Some can be life-threatening, weakening the bodyâs natural defenses while also damaging healthy tissue.

The part of the body under attack determines which signs appear, so the picture varies dramatically from one cat to the next. Signs are often general and not immediately linked to an autoimmune problem. Possible signs include lethargy, weakness, decreased appetite, weight loss, vomiting, diarrhea, and other changes to the feces. Other signs include pale gums, unexplained bleeding, bruising, a high temperature, a faster heart rate, rapid breathing, and tiring easily during play. Limping, swollen joints, sores on the skin, a poor coat, increased thirst, increased urination, and collapse are also on the list.

Many of these signs come and go. A cat may seem completely fine one day and be struggling the next. Any of these signs are a reason to contact a vet promptly, since starting investigations early makes a real difference.

The problem starts with the catâs own immune system, which is part of what makes it so hard to manage. The exact triggers are not fully understood, and more research is needed. An immune-mediated condition can be triggered when the immune system is stimulated. Viral infections, vaccination, medications, inherited conditions, and hormones have all been linked to autoimmune disease. Where things get tricky is that the incident that triggered the disease is not usually the cause of the disease. That means that the same type of autoimmune disease can be triggered by different things in different individuals.

Some of these conditions can be passed down, and other possible contributors include poor nutrition, toxins, cancer, and stress. A cat diagnosed with an autoimmune disease considered hereditary should not be bred under any circumstances.

Diagnosis is often a process of elimination. More common causes of the same signs are investigated and ruled out first, which can mean lots of tests. A vet will start with a full physical exam and a detailed history, then choose tests to fit the signs. These may include blood and urine tests, X-rays, or an ultrasound scan. Samples may also be taken from wounds, rashes, or other affected areas. Prompt diagnosis is important for effective management.

Treatment depends on the type of autoimmune disease, what part of the body is affected, and what is behind it, so it varies greatly. The usual approach is medication that calms the immune system so it stops attacking the body, most often corticosteroids like prednisone or dexamethasone. A large starting dose is often given, and once the signs settle down, the dose is lowered gradually.

Sometimes, stronger immunosuppressive drugs are also used. Some of them are also used in cancer treatment, and the side effects tend to be more severe. The trouble with treating an immune-mediated disease is that we are using medication to suppress the immune system, and that leaves the patient more susceptible to infection and other diseases.

Even more challenging when treating feline autoimmune disease is that some of the medications used to treat dogs or humans are toxic to cats.

Supportive care matters just as much. Pain relief is often needed, but many commonly prescribed anti-inflammatory painkillers cannot be given alongside steroids. Stronger painkillers like opioids or gabapentin may be used to manage severe pain, and these come with their own side effects. Antibiotics may also be prescribed, either prophylactically if extremely high doses of immunosuppressive medications are being used, or if secondary infections develop.

For cats with immune-mediated hemolytic anemia (IMHA), where the immune system is attacking and destroying red blood cells, or immune-mediated thrombocytopenia (IMTP) where platelets are being destroyed, blood or plasma transfusions may be needed to save a catâs life.

Lifestyle changes can help too. A cat with painful, swollen joints, for example, benefits from losing weight if overweight, and lower sides on litter trays and softer beds make life more comfortable. Each case is assessed individually.

One of the more common autoimmune diseases in cats is known as Eosinophilic Granuloma Complex, a group of idiopathic conditions that are currently quite poorly understood. The exact cause of the disease is unknown; however, parasites, allergies, and genetic factors have been identified as likely triggers.

There are three main clinical presentations of eosinophilic granuloma complex:

Ulcerative: Also known as indolent ulcers or rodent ulcers, this form of eosinophilic granuloma complex usually presents with a sore on the cat's mouth. It is typically seen on the upper lip. They are normally red in color and do not appear to cause your cat any pain or irritation.

Plaque-forming: often found on the stomach and on the inside of the hind legs. The lesions are often itchy and red and often become infected, which complicates matters.

Granuloma forming: usually presents as raised pink or yellow nodules that can be located anywhere on your cat.

Another more common feline immune-mediated disease is called pemphigus foliaceus. It affects the skin, causing scabs, crusts, and sores around the eyes and ears, on the paws, and in the groin area. When it develops on the paws, the nail beds can be affected as well.

There is no cure for autoimmune disease, but effective treatment and management can control the signs, greatly improve quality of life, and allow most cats to live fairly comfortably. These conditions can be debilitating and life-threatening, yet it is often possible to manage them well enough for cats to lead relatively normal lives. Because the signs overlap with so many other conditions, a vet visit at the first sign that something is wrong is the surest way to get answers early.

Archaeologists discovered hundreds of stone hammers at a Spanish copper mine that likely traded its metal with Scandinavia

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Excavations at Las Minillas in Badajoz found 735 grooved stone hammers and 17 radiocarbon dates, and 11 Nordic bronzes fit its copper.

Digging at Las Minillas, a Bronze Age copper mine just south of Granja de Torrehermosa in Badajoz province, Spain, started in 2020 as a joint effort of the University of Seville and Gothenburg’s Maritime Encounters project. Field campaigns in 2020, 2021 and 2023 logged 892 stone tools, and 735 were grooved stone hammers.

Then came the lead isotope results. Slag and tiny copper droplets from the mine gave lead isotope values that fit 11 bronzes from hoards and other sites in Denmark and Sweden.

The paper, by Juan Latorre-Ruiz of Durham University and nine co-authors, went online on October 26, 2026. It calls those bronzes consistent with Las Minillas copper, not proven to come from it. It also says the mine produced less copper than Mitterberg in the Austrian Alps.

The outcrop is about 985 feet (300 meters) long. Claude Domergue put the old workings in his 1987 catalogue of ancient mines and smelters of the Iberian Peninsula. Mark Hunt Ortiz of the University of Seville, now a co-author, published a surface survey of the mine in 2017.

The work began as a forerunner of Maritime Encounters, a University of Gothenburg program led by archaeology professor Johan Ling. In 2021 Sweden’s Riksbankens Jubileumsfond gave that program 47 million Swedish kronor.

Field crews ran magnetometer and ground-penetrating radar surveys and then opened 11 trenches. A Rolatec RL-36-L rotary rig drilled into the old workings, and drill hole S3, near trench 8, brought up charcoal from 27 feet (8.2 meters) down.

Bronze Age miners had cut one long open trench along the vein, 6.5 to 13 feet (2 to 4 meters) wide. It is now packed with rubble, some of it probably dumped during underground mining in the early 20th century.

Trench 12, first called “trench pond” in the supplementary report, is the only place where the dig reached the bottom of the old cut, and no ore was left there.

Burnt and mineralized wood at the base suggests fire-setting, where miners lit fires against the rock so that it would crack.

In trench 3 the team found postholes, fireplaces and the remains of a round hut, with handmade burnished pottery of a style known from southwestern Iberia. The supplementary report reads it as a short-lived hut, probably part of a seasonal mining camp.

Marta Díaz-Guardamino of Durham photographed the hammers. They look like the hammers from Chinflón, the first prehistoric mine studied in Iberia, in the 1970s.

Based on the tools, the dates and the size of the workings, the team calls Las Minillas the largest securely dated Late Bronze Age copper mine excavated in Iberia so far.

Until this project, 11 radiocarbon dates from five sites backed Late Bronze Age copper mining in Iberia. Las Minillas added 17, run on charcoal from strawberry tree, oak, mastic and other plants.

Nine samples came from the mining trench and eight from the hut. They fall between about 1350 and 750 BC, and the authors place copper production at the site from about 1300 to 800 or 700 BC.

Copper mining in Iberia goes back much further. A high cave in the eastern Pyrenees has given evidence of copper mining going back 7,000 years.

The Las Minillas dates line up with a gap elsewhere in Europe. Great Orme in Wales was in decline by 1400 BC, and the Mitterberg mines in Austria followed around 1300 to 1200 BC, according to the paper.

Zofia Stos-Gale of the University of Gothenburg drew up the isotope plots. The team thanks the SGIker isotope facility of the University of the Basque Country for technical support.

Each ore body carries its own mix of lead isotopes, and bronze made from it keeps that mix. The team checked Las Minillas against published data on more than 300 Late Bronze Age bronzes from Scandinavia.

Eleven objects matched in isotopes and in their silver and antimony levels. A rod and a pair of tweezers from Tillinge in Uppland, the oldest of the 11, date to between 1300 and 900 BC.

Two hanging bowls came from Danish hoards, one on Funen and one near Aalborg, and a spearhead came from Orust in western Sweden. A socketed axe and a disc-headed pin came from a scrap hoard of 47 objects in Uppland.

Two twisted neck rings from a hoard at Antvorskov, near Slagelse, date to 700 to 500 BC, after the mine shut. Seven of the objects date to 900 to 700 BC, the same years as the last work at Las Minillas.

Iberian copper has also been traced in bronze figurines from Sardinia and in ingots from the Salcombe metal cargoes off Devon, in southern England.

A 2025 chapter by Hunt Ortiz and colleagues put the mine’s copper at about 217 tons (197 metric tons) as a cautious figure, with a range from 54 to 814 tons (49 to 738 metric tons). Great Orme is thought to have produced 223 to 833 tons (202 to 756 metric tons) between 1600 and 1400 BC.

Mitterberg produced about 25,000 tons (23,000 metric tons) between about 1700 and 1300 BC. Surveys have listed 84 sites in southwestern Iberia with hammers like the Las Minillas ones, and the paper cites more than 100 possible prehistoric mines across southern Iberia.

Las Minillas first came up in an earlier look at how Spanish metal reached Scandinavia, which followed a survey from February 9 to 16, 2026, around Cabeza del Buey, also in Badajoz.

Ling’s group ran it with the University of Seville and the provincial archaeology museum and found six unregistered Bronze Age mines, one with about 80 grooved stone tools. The new paper adds the excavation record and the 11 matching bronzes.

Ling said the discovery of the new mines in Extremadura “represents only the tip of the iceberg,” in a University of Gothenburg release. He put the number of prehistoric mines still undocumented in Extremadura and Andalusia at up to 150.

Trenches 6 and 7 held copper droplets and a partly preserved round structure of stone and mud brick. In 2022 the crew collected all the slag in trench 6, which weighed about 660 pounds (300 kilograms).

Rodents, ploughing and the removal of mine waste for ballast have mixed up the soil there. Five charcoal samples from the area dated to the Roman period or to between AD 1023 and 1158.

A reddened clay fragment from the structure was dated by luminescence to about 700 BC, plus or minus 270 years. The structure could be a pottery kiln rather than a smelting furnace, the supplementary report says.

The paper says the droplets and slag indicate smelting took place, though the round structure may not have been a furnace.

Only six mines in southern Iberia are firmly dated to the Late Bronze Age, so most of the mines behind the regional picture still have no dates.

A full site report will compare Las Minillas metal with bronzes from Britain, Poland, Spain and Portugal. The 735 hammers stay at the center of the case for large-scale mining at Granja de Torrehermosa.

The full study was published in the journal Antiquity.

Here’s what happened in crypto today

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Need to know what happened in crypto today? Here is the latest news on daily trends and events impacting Bitcoin price, blockchain, DeFi, Web3 and crypto regulation.

Today in crypto, Democrats secured control of the US Senate in the midterm elections, potentially reshaping the outlook for crypto legislation and regulatory oversight. Meanwhile, prediction markets saw record midterm trading volumes as traders wagered on control of Congress, and Kraken restricted accounts linked to a suspected $133 million crypto theft involving an unidentified European Union entity.

Democrats have won control of the US Senate after flipping Republican-held seats in Texas, Ohio, North Carolina, South Carolina, Kansas and Iowa, according to the Associated Press.

The majority was secured after Democrat Senator Josh Turek won the Senate race for Iowa, bringing Democrats and independents who caucus with them to 51 seats.

The result reshapes the outlook for the stalled CLARITY Act and determines which lawmakers lead committees overseeing the industry.

Democratic control could also bring more congressional scrutiny of Trump-linked crypto businesses and regulators’ decisions. Control of the Senate could also affect confirmation of future presidential nominees to regulatory posts.

As Americans across the country head to the polls for the US midterm elections Tuesday, prediction market traders have spent months wagering on the outcome. According to an analysis by The Washington Post, nearly $1.9 billion in election-related contracts had traded on Kalshi and Polymarket as of Monday, marking record trading activity for a midterm election cycle.

Markets on both platforms favor Democrats winning control of Congress, with a Democratic sweep priced at 75% on Kalshi and 75.5% on Polymarket as of 4:30 pm UTC. Kalshi traders gave Democrats a 93% chance of taking the House and a 75% chance of winning the Senate, while Polymarket priced those outcomes at 94.5% and 75.5%, respectively.

The vote comes nine days after prediction markets failed to anticipate the outcome of Brazil’s presidential election, with Flávio Bolsonaro favored at roughly 80% the day before incumbent President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva won the runoff. Kalshi CEO Tarek Mansour reportedly cautioned that “a 5% probability doesn’t mean it won’t happen,” according to CNBC.

For the crypto industry, a shift in congressional control could reshape the prospects for long-awaited regulatory clarity.

“This wasn’t one mistake or one breach — it was a months-long campaign,” Binance co-CEO Richard Teng said in a Tuesday post on X.

Kraken has restricted accounts linked to a suspected crypto theft of more than $133 million from an unidentified European Union entity and is working with law enforcement to support recovery efforts.

“This incident did not originate on the Kraken platform, and we’re actively investigating reports that some of the assets were subsequently moved through accounts on our platform,” Kraken told Cointelegraph. “We’ve restricted the accounts in question and are working with law enforcement to support recovery efforts.”

Kraken’s statement follows blockchain investigator ZachXBT’s disclosure on Tuesday that he identified another $27 million in withdrawals from centralized exchanges, increasing the suspected losses from the $106 million he reported on Monday. He said some withdrawals occurred after his initial disclosure.

Bitquery reported that the suspected stolen funds were withdrawn from accounts at Kraken, Crypto.com and OKX. It also found that $62.98 million in stablecoins was converted into 27,553 Ether (ETH) within roughly four hours.

Crypto.com told Cointelegraph that it had taken steps to prevent funds associated with the incident from moving through its platform.

OKX acknowledged Cointelegraph’s request for comment and said it was looking into the matter.

Cointelegraph is committed to providing independent, high-quality journalism across the crypto, blockchain, AI, and fintech industries.

All news, reviews, and analyses are produced with full journalistic independence and integrity. For more details on our standards and processes, please read our Editorial Policy.
